# Trend ledger — Quantum AI Radar

Last updated: 2026-11-14

Stage legend: `seed` (first signal) → `emerging` (multi-source, forming) →
`accelerating` (broad, fast) → `mainstreaming` (standard practice) ; `dormant`
(21+ days quiet) ; `declining` (the field moved on). Confidence: `low | medium | high`.
Trend bar: ≥3 independent sources (different orgs/author groups) + ≥1 concrete artifact.

## Active trends

### [id: trend-001] Practical QEC tooling: near-term error detection and the path to FTQC

alias: practical-qec-tooling
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-26
last_evidence: 2026-11-10
evidence:
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13630 — Barter, Bridgeman, Dawson, Farrelly: "Machine-learning decoders for fusion-based quantum computing in real time" — PRISM, a transformer-based ML decoder for photonic FUSION-BASED architectures, built to exploit the known locations of erasures from photon loss and probabilistic fusion measurements (a dominant error mechanism this trend's prior evidence, all superconducting/qLDPC-focused, had not yet covered); benchmarked across a wide range of fault-tolerant fusion-based protocols — a new independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep, and the trend's first PHOTONIC/fusion-based-platform decoder; displaces Shtanko/Roy/Kumar/Minev's IBM Virtual Error Cancellation paper (2610.12400, Oct-8, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its full-stack dual-decoder classical-post-processing mitigation finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-09-21 history below
  - 2026-11-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06819 — Batista, Falcao, Machado (opened this session via today's arXiv sweep): "GFlowQEC: Generative Flow Networks that Learn to Sample Quantum LDPC Codes" — the first generative flow network (GFlowNet) for quantum code design, learning to propose quantum LDPC codes built from a few group elements in proportion to a reward that scores circuit-level-simulated performance, making every costly simulation count under a tight per-evaluation search budget — a 14th independent group (CAP-SWAP), extending the trend's ML-decoder tooling to ML-driven CODE DISCOVERY specifically; displaces Higgott/Sayginel/Heras et al.'s Google Quantum AI/DeepMind/MIT boundary-connected-planar-modules paper (2610.03682, Oct-2, a capture-leak catch added 2026-11-04, now the trend's longest-resident item at the point of this swap) — no information lost, its 10x+ physical-qubit-overhead-reduction finding via hyperbolic-tiling boundary couplers remains summarized in this trend's 2026-11-04 capture-leak history
  - 2026-10-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17156 — Sayedsalehi, Bagherzadeh, Shcherbakov, Gaudiot (May-2026 preprint, now npj Quantum Information-published, publicized via a Quantum Computing Report digest, opened this session): "Sparse Mamba Decoder for Quantum Error Correction: Efficient Defect-Centric Processing of Surface Code Syndromes" — a Mamba state-space decoder cuts decoding complexity from O(d⁴) to O(d²) in code distance, matching AlphaQubit-style Transformer-decoder accuracy on Google Sycamore benchmarks with a lower simulated logical error rate and higher effective threshold at distance 9 — a 13th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-29 (across 5 dailies)
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41398 — Bilgin, Hou, Srinivas, You: "Distilling Knowledge from Search: Decoder Model Factory for Quantum LDPC Codes" — GraphMP, a learning-based message-passing decoder for qLDPC codes that transfers WITHOUT fine-tuning across code families, sizes, physical error rates and noise models; introduces a "decoder model factory" that trains successive decoder generations on model-guided-search-verified trajectories (knowledge distillation from beam search), improving the decoder each generation as a coupled training/decoding loop rather than a one-shot training run — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; displaces Astolfi & Pupillo's POLYMECHANON code-agnostic GNN decoder (2610.01683, Oct-1, added 2026-10-02, the trend's longest-resident item at ~29 days) — no information lost, its DEM-based any-code/any-noise-model decoding result remains summarized in this trend's 2026-10-02 history below
  - 2026-10-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40490 — Hu, Wang, Luo, Xiang, Zhao: "Fast and accurate neural triage decoding of ultra-high-rate quantum LDPC codes" — introduces "neural triage decoding," a framework using syndrome consistency to adaptively allocate neural computation (a fast local message-passing network handles easy syndromes, escalating harder ones to slower accurate decoding), the first neural decoder demonstrated for high-rate qLDPC codes (>1/2 encoding rate, ~1000 physical data qubits) — an 11th independent group on this trend's decoder-tooling sub-theme; CAP-SWAP, displacing the 2026-09-30 Graph Neural Post-selection item (2610.00504, thematically overlapping with already-retained GNN-decoder evidence)
  - 2026-10-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32347 — Harper, Kolesnikow, McLauchlan, Nixon, Bartlett, Brown (University of Sydney): "Entangled logical qubits on a superconducting quantum processor" — high-fidelity logical-qubit operations on `ibm_phoenix` (120 coupled superconducting qubits, fast reset), routinely producing distance-3/distance-5 surface codes with logical error per round below 1%; demonstrates entanglement between two logical qubits via lattice surgery (Bell-state fidelities above 93%) and, with three logical qubits, logical teleportation of a range of states/gates (78-93% fidelities), plus a 170-round stability experiment reaching ~2e-7
  - 2026-10-19 — https://nvidia.github.io/cuda-quantum/blogs/blog/2026/10/19/jupiter-magic-state-simulation/ — Svore et al. (NVIDIA + Jülich Supercomputing Centre, technical blog, opened this session, held since 2026-10-20 as the standing flagged cap-swap candidate): "Advancing Fault-Tolerant Quantum Computing with GPU-Accelerated Simulation" — simulates magic-state preparation in a 104-qubit protocol using 8,192 GPUs on the JUPITER supercomputer, development/validation supported by the NVIDIA Accelerated Quantum Research Center (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23840 — Kan, Hoyt, Garner, Vo, Harkness, Rofougaran, Du, Liu, Tubman, Mao, Stein: "Demonstration of Logical Error Suppression in a Surface Code Memory on a Cloud-Accessible IBM Superconducting Processor" — an independent (non-IBM-internal) team studies rotated surface-code memory on an IBM Nighthawk r2 processor accessed over the cloud, characterizing how device variation, noise asymmetry and spatiotemporal correlations complicate translating below-threshold physical noise into exponential logical-error suppression in practice, then optimizes placement/configuration to recover it — resolves the standing flagged cap-swap candidate held since 2026-10-19, and the first evidence item from a THIRD-PARTY team (not IBM itself) validating logical-error suppression on IBM's own cloud-accessible hardware (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-15 — https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/models-and-research/quantum-computing/magic-state-cultivation-quantum-computing — Rosenfeld, Gidney (Google Quantum AI, technical blog, opened this session), publicizing "Magic state cultivation on a superconducting quantum processor" (published today in Science, DOI 10.1126/science.aee7961): the first physical hardware implementation of magic state cultivation on the Willow processor, reducing T-state error 40x versus no cultivation and reaching 99.99(1)% logical fidelity (retaining 8% of attempts), including code-switching into a surface code and a fault-tolerant measurement protocol to bound magic-state fidelity — a flagship hardware demonstration directly addressing the resource bottleneck (non-Clifford T-gates) this trend's own "path to FTQC" framing centers on (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-28 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01403-y — Lee, Hur, Park (npj Quantum Information, peer-reviewed, opened this session via today's npj QI research-articles sweep — the listing's own curl-recoverable heal held up this run, metadata via Crossref): "Scalable neural decoders for practical real-time quantum error correction" — replaces the O(d^4)-scaling attention mechanism in Transformer-based decoders (e.g. AlphaQubit) with a scalable architecture, aimed at the real-time-decoding-at-scale bottleneck this trend's tooling evidence repeatedly returns to — a fresh independent group (CAP-SWAP), a ~15-day backlog catch; displaces Riverlane's decoder-recalibration-speedup blog post (2026-10-14, the trend's oldest remaining item, a vendor technical blog rather than a peer-reviewed research artifact) — no information lost, its NVIDIA-H100 real-time recalibration speedup remains summarized on `study_shelf`
notes: Ten independent groups now on the QEC tooling axis (USTC/Zhu-Pan experimental group, IBM, foundation-decoder group, QLDPC code designers, Paris-Saclay decoder-benchmark group, MIT-CTP/neutral-atom QLDPC group, Yale/Puri biased-noise-ancilla group, readout-error-coding group, MIT/Chuang ML-decoding group, erasure-postselection group). IBM explicitly frames Paulice as a bridge between error mitigation and fault tolerance. Foundation decoders and the MIT/Chuang Quantum-journal result show neural/ML decoding approaches now span both large code distances and specific code families (bivariate bicycle) at a peer-reviewed level. Benois et al. demonstrate practical decoders fall 5× short of optimality, motivating better decoder design; Zhao et al. extend ultra-high-rate QLDPC codes to neutral-atom hardware; Bi et al. tackle QLDPC syndrome-extraction circuit design; the readout-coding group covers the measurement-error layer; Griffiths et al. quantify the practical limits of the same postselection technique class IBM shipped in Paulice. Stage promoted emerging → accelerating 2026-07-01: 10 independent groups in under 3 weeks is broad AND fast, clearing the "accelerating" bar flagged as a candidate in the 2026-06-30 report and held one run for confirmation. ### [id: trend-002] AI-for-quantum (hardware leg): classical ML for quantum hardware control, calibration, decoding and circuit design

alias: ai-for-quantum-hardware
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-26
last_evidence: 2026-11-03
evidence:
  - 2026-10-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36167 — Shi, Liu, Peng: "DiffSimuQ: Automatic Differentiation for Analog Quantum Experiments" — shows a broad class of open-loop analog quantum experiments (executable programs returning only measurement samples) is closed under automatic differentiation, giving a general gradient-based optimization method for analog quantum hardware without finite-difference noise sensitivity — a 21st independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-28 (across 8+ dailies); displaces Tong/Shirizly/Chen/Wang/Pokharel's dynamic-circuit error-suppression-learning paper (2604.18734, added 2026-09-29, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its mid-circuit-measurement-and-feedforward dynamical-decoupling-learning finding remains summarized in this trend's W29-split-era history above. Today's arXiv sweep also surfaced Rossignolo/Bianchet/Saha Roy/Machnes's differentiable-digital-twin paper for neutral-atom/ion gate calibration (2611.09779, Nov-6) — a closely related differentiable-control result, queued as the trend's next candidate rather than forcing a 2nd same-day swap.
  - 2026-11-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04179 — Moradi, Nourozi, Tang, Mitchell: "Hardware-Efficient Reinforcement-Learned Scheduling for Quantum LDPC Decoders" — hardware implementations (BP, RL-S, and bucket-based reinforcement-learned BP/BRL-S) for the [[288,12,18]] bivariate bicycle qLDPC code, fixing the heap-based priority-management hardware overhead that otherwise blocks RL-learned adaptive variable-node scheduling from reaching an ASIC/FPGA decoder — a 20th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep, extending the trend's own decoder-HARDWARE-design strand (alongside IBM's agent-factories entry) from the decoding algorithm to a concrete RL-decoder ASIC/FPGA implementation
  - 2026-10-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29170 — Zhong, Ren, Zheng, Zeng, Zheng, Li, Liang: "Turning Quantum Compiler Configuration Selection Upside Down: A Top-Down Paradigm" — replaces the SOTA bottom-up (device-then-RL-tuned-compiler-options) strategy with a top-down paradigm that jointly reasons over hardware-target and compiler-setting choices, avoiding being bottlenecked by an early, poorly-matched device decision — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-22
  - 2026-10-21 — https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/quantum-computing/decoding-quantum-errors-with-nvidia-ising-decoding-on-aws/ — Huang, Reshef, Rodenbach, Pakrasi, Takeshita (AWS + NVIDIA, technical blog, opened this session): trains and benchmarks NVIDIA's open-source Ising Decoding AI pre-decoder across five accelerated EC2 instance types (A100/H100/H200/B200/L40S GPUs) on AWS Parallel Computing Service, then validates it on a distance-9-to-31 surface-code sweep — at distance 31 the pre-decoder cuts per-round decoding latency 2.4x (Fast) to 2.9x (Accurate) versus MWPM alone while improving the logical error rate — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a 3rd organization (AWS) independently corroborating the AI-pre-decoder theme already touched by NVIDIA's own release and PennyLane's Backline integration, with concrete cross-GPU-generation training-cost/latency benchmarks
  - 2026-10-16 (undated on the page, accessed this session) — https://research.ibm.com/blog/open-agent-factories-and-distributed-inference-for-chip-design — IBM Research (technical blog, opened this session), publicizing "Highly Cost-Efficient Agent Factories for High Level Synthesis: Design Space Exploration for Quantum Error Correction Chip with Open Frontier Models and Distributed Inference at Scale" (Bhandwaldar, Choudhury, Puri et al.): AI agent factories (a coordinator model plus parallel worker agents proposing architectural transformations) automate the design-space exploration of High-Level-Synthesis C++ hardware implementations of the Relay-BP qLDPC decoder for the [[144,12,12]] bivariate-bicycle code; an all-open-weight configuration (GLM-5.3 + llm-d distributed inference) reached a 52x hardware speedup for ~$26 in inference cost (11.5x cheaper than an all-proprietary Claude-Opus configuration at $299), while the proprietary configuration reached a larger 204x speedup — a 15th independent group, extending the trend's "decoding and circuit design" framing from the decoding ALGORITHM to the decoder's own classical ASIC/FPGA HARDWARE design process (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02129 — Dillon, Javed, Harkin, Dabkowski, Lienhard: "Spiking neural networks for streaming qubit readout" — spiking-neural-network discriminators process superconducting-qubit readout traces in successive time chunks, updating the state estimate as data streams in rather than waiting for the full readout window; outperforms matched-filter discrimination and approaches full-trace ANN accuracy, with quantisation-aware training + hls4ml synthesis demonstrating each FPGA inference update completes before the next readout chunk arrives — a 14th independent group, extending the trend's control/calibration/decoding/design surface to the real-time streaming-readout layer
  - 2026-09-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31977 — Aktar, Tate, Eidenbenz: "Learning Trotter Orderings for Heisenberg Hamiltonians with a Ranking Transformer" — learns the noncommuting-term ordering for Trotterized Hamiltonian simulation directly with a physics-aware ranking transformer, replacing the factorial-search-space trial-and-error prior approaches need — a 13th independent group (CAP-SWAP), directly pairing with the trend's own RL-Trotter evidence (2608.20139), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-09-27
  - 2026-10-13 — https://pennylane.ai/blog/2026/10/bring-your-own-decoder-backline — Izaac, Malek, O'Riordan (Xanadu/PennyLane, technical blog; a capture-leak catch, opened this session, missed by 3 consecutive dailies): "Bring your own decoder: hierarchical QEC decoding with Backline, NVIDIA Ising, and PyMatching" — Backline, a new PennyLane tool letting a hierarchical QEC pipeline mix decoders (neural-network, compiled-library, or plain Python) from a single program; demonstrated by running NVIDIA's Ising AI pre-decoder on an AMD Instinct GPU paired with PyMatching for online decoding, end-to-end from one Python program — a 16th independent group, the first item on this trend pairing a software-integration framework with an AI pre-decoder specifically (CAP-SWAP, displaces the Q-CTRL Fire Opal Nighthawk-speedup post, whose claim is already substantiated by an older arXiv benchmark (2605.04025) already summarized elsewhere in this trend's history — no information lost)
  - 2026-10-15 — https://firstqfm.com/news (FirstQFM, intake-sourced technical press release, opened this session): "FirstQFM Foundation Model Delivers 33% Shorter Gate Times on QuantWare QPU Using NVIDIA Accelerated Computing" — a quantum-foundation-model vendor's gate/calibration-optimization model, hardware-validated on a real QuantWare QPU (33% shorter gate times) using NVIDIA accelerated computing — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-16; FirstQFM promoted from Discovered-source candidates into this trend's own evidence
  - 2026-07-08 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10759-2 — Google Quantum AI (peer-reviewed in Nature 2026): "Reinforcement learning control of quantum error correction" — an RL agent unifies device calibration with error correction, continuously learning from live QEC/syndrome data to keep the processor calibrated DURING a computation (avoiding the full-stop recalibration that is incompatible with long fault-tolerant runtimes) and lowering the logical error rate of a surface code on real hardware; a flagship hardware-control-leg result

### [id: trend-009] LLM/agentic AI reasoning about quantum circuits, algorithms and proofs

alias: llm-agentic-quantum-reasoning
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-18
last_evidence: 2026-11-06
evidence:
  - 2026-11-06 — https://psi.inc/research/aaronson-ambainis/ + https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09773 — Turturean, Halverson et al. (Physical Superintelligence, official research blog + preprint, opened this session via today's Scott Aaronson-blog/TQI-digest sweep): "A proof of the Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture" — PSI's own research system, running instances of a non-public Anthropic model on PSI's infrastructure, found and Lean-verified a full proof of the 2008 Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture (every bounded degree-d polynomial on the Boolean cube with constant variance has an influential variable), independently reviewed by Guy Blanc (MIT) and Jordan Docter/Carmen Strassle/Li-Yang Tan (Stanford) and publicly confirmed correct by Scott Aaronson himself — settling a 17-year-old central open problem in quantum query complexity (superpolynomial quantum query advantage over classical, on random/unstructured inputs, requires a promise/structure, exactly as AA anticipated); same org (PSI) already in this trend's evidence via the 2026-10-23 "Five results" item, so not a NEW independent group, but a vastly more significant result from that group — displaces haiqu.ai's "Hubbard Model on Real Quantum Hardware" case study (added 2026-10-09, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its diagonal-hopping-Hubbard-model physics result remains summarized in this trend's 2026-10-09 history below (CAP-SWAP). HYPE-SKEPTIC NOTE (hard rule): this result does NOT "kill quantum supremacy," despite viral social-media claims to that effect (explicitly rebutted by Aaronson on his own blog) — it only rules out exponential quantum query advantage for DECISION problems on RANDOM oracles, a case already widely conjectured false; it has zero bearing on Shor's algorithm (non-random/periodic structure), quantum chemistry/physics simulation, or any advantage claim resting on a genuine promise/structure.
  - 2026-11-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09822 — Shirobokov, Jaderberg, Hristova, Tsoukalas, Bausch, Wieser, Heras, Rubin, Low, Racanière, Tomašev, Zhang, Watson, Khattar, Albarghouthi, Jain, Khan, Sarra, Senior, Broughton, Masoom, Boixo, Babbush, Chaudhuri, Kohli (Google Quantum AI + collaborators, opened this session via today's arXiv sweep): "Discovering formally verified quantum programs with AI" — large multi-agent LLM teams synthesize novel quantum programs directly in the Lean theorem prover against formal problem specifications, verifying both correctness AND cost, sharing results/knowledge through wikis and discussing ideas at organized agent "conferences"; demonstrates order-of-magnitude improvements beyond prior human-expert baselines (up to 1.4x on parallel search, 290x on quantum arithmetic, 11x/8x/2.5x on fusion/photochemistry/drug-metabolism-relevant simulations) — same Google Quantum AI team already in this trend's evidence via Khattar/Rubin/Gidney/Broughton/Low/Babbush's 2610.23832 (added 2026-10-16), so a REFRESH of that team's own evidence rather than a new independent group, but a dramatically larger-scale successor result (a full agent-collaboration research library, not a single compilation technique); displaces that 2610.23832 entry (added 2026-10-16, same org) — no information lost, its verifiable-structure circuit-compilation finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-10-16 history below (CAP-SWAP).
  - 2026-11-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02833 — Du, Zhao, Wang, Lu, Zhang, Tao (opened this session via today's Quantum Zeitgeist digest sweep, traced to the primary): "Reimagining computational quantum research with AI scientists" — introduces OmniQ, an expert-audited virtual research environment that converts published computational-quantum-science papers into executable reproduction tasks, benchmarking AI scientists' ability to turn the quantum scientific record into a foundation for autonomous discovery; systematically evaluated 59 papers and found current AI systems frequently fail to reproduce published results — a 25th independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's first dedicated reproducibility-benchmarking infrastructure for AI-scientist agents specifically in computational quantum science; displaces Song/Zhang/Zhang/Wang/Ma/Du's QuSema silent-bug-detection-agent paper (2610.10258, Oct-7, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its quantum-knowledge-enhanced LLM-agent bug-detection finding remains summarized in this trend's history above
  - 2026-10-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34841 — Li, Li, Zhao: "Large Language Models for Quantum Code Translation: An Exploratory Study" — an LLM-based quantum-aware translation workflow between high-level quantum SDKs (e.g. Qiskit↔Cirq), with prompt design and validation reflecting quantum semantics — a 24th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-27 (across 4 dailies); opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; displaces Dulal/Grandadam/Koch-Janusz/Maksymenko's Quantum Agentic Operating System (QAOS) infrastructure paper (2610.05304, Oct-4, added 2026-10-04, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its nonlinear-workflow-graph multi-agent-research-system finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-10-04 history below
  - 2026-10-23 — https://psi.inc/research/oqp-batch-2026/ — PSI (Physical Superintelligence, official research blog, opened this session): "Five results on Open Quantum Problems" — PSI's own AI research system obtains new results on five open problems in quantum information theory (an entanglement-distillability bound for Werner states, a CHSH-violation locality bound for vacuum correlations, optimal CGLMP-violating measurement strategies, a tight bound on how many PPT maps suffice to break entanglement, and a counterexample to a causal-process-extension conjecture), each released with a paper, a Lean formalization and verification code — a 23rd independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's most direct instance yet of an AI system DOING quantum-information-theory research itself rather than reasoning about circuits/code; psi.inc staged as a new discovered source below
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31352 — Crichigno, Flammia: "Automating quantum circuit optimization with AI agents" — builds on Open-Autoresearch-style AI-agent loops (the same methodology family as this trend's own ECDSA.Fail evidence) for compiling quantum algorithms onto real devices, adding a frozen exact verifier that certifies every result PLUS a 2nd agent that builds and validates that verifier (against a solved case, deliberately-broken solutions, and published results) before any candidate solution exists; applied to three quantum-simulation compilation problems — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 18 prior
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21942 — Wang, Guo, Li, Li, Wang, Wang: "Jianmu-Q: Bridging Quantum Field Theory and Quantum Hardware with AI Agents" — a multi-agent framework (specialized agents for theoretical formulation, lattice discretization, qubit mapping, circuit design, physical-consistency checking) translates natural-language descriptions of quantum field theories directly into quantum simulation workflows — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing STRONG-flagged queue candidate held since 2026-10-15
  - 2026-10-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12187 — Brown, Nguyen, Chatterjee, Lubinski: "Autonomous Code Migration for Quantum Programming Languages: A Case Study with QED-C Benchmarks" — Opus, GPT and Gemini coding agents each ported 13 QED-C benchmark methods from Qiskit to CUDA-Q with little direct help; model-generated "skills" distilled from the exercise cut required model turns by up to 85% and token usage by up to 96% on an unseen benchmark, and no single model was consistently best — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a concrete, quantified multi-LLM agentic-coding result for quantum software specifically
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32001 — Nys, Jreissaty, Carrasquilla: "QuantumMatterExplorer: An Autonomous Agent for the Discovery of Quantum Many-Body Phases of Matter" — an LLM-based autonomous agent that takes only a Hamiltonian and parameter ranges and returns a phase diagram from its own numerical simulations, reasoning over a long horizon with a persistent written plan — a 22nd independent group (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13811 — Jeon, Lee, Kim et al.: "QTestBench: A Test Framework for Evaluating LLM-Generated Quantum Code" — the first benchmark measuring not whether an LLM-written quantum program is correct, but whether the LLM-written UNIT TESTS accompanying it can actually distinguish correct implementations from faulty ones, given that quantum programs are probabilistic and judged by domain-specific numerical-tolerance checkers that can disagree with a well-formed classical-style test — a 21st independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving a queue item that had sat past the mandatory 14-day queue-residency line (17 days) despite sitting squarely on this trend's own axis

### [id: trend-003] Quantum generative models: circuits for generative and sequential learning

alias: quantum-generative-ml
stage: emerging
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-29
last_evidence: 2026-11-10
evidence:
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13671 — Wijayasiri, Bacarreza, Clements, Fok: "Quantum-Assisted Generative Anomaly Detection: A Dimension-Controlled Study of Latent Refinement" — compares a 512-dimensional classical Gaussian latent prior against a 48-dimensional quantum-sampled prior (and a matching 48-dimensional classical Gaussian control) inside SAGEGAN, a style-based generative anomaly detector trained only on benign data, applied to malware detection — a 22nd independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep, extending the trend's generative-modeling evidence into a new cybersecurity/anomaly-detection application domain with a controlled quantum-vs-classical-latent ablation; displaces Sekwao/De/Hocken/Staniewicz/Epifanovsky/Stringham's SAR/InSAR change-detection paper (2609.05313, Sep-3, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its IonQ-hardware-executed satellite-radar-change-detection finding remains summarized in this trend's history below
  - 2026-11-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06630 — Ngo, Nguyen, Seo, Xing, Thai (opened this session via today's quant-ph∩cs.LG cross-list sweep): "Noise-regularized training of quantum circuit Born machines under a finite shot budget" — shows that for ANY noise process, a QCBM's generalization gap (trained with maximum mean discrepancy) is bounded by the complexity of the distributions the noisy model can reach; scrambled local Pauli noise contracts this complexity but biases the model toward the uniform distribution, so residual noise acts as a REGULARIZER whose optimal strength balances expressivity against overfitting — a 21st independent group (CAP-SWAP), a theory result directly on the trend's own QCBM-training axis; displaces Sanz Larrarte/Dastbasteh/Sanchez-Navarro et al.'s in-silico-clinical-trials quantum generative model (2608.28168, Aug-28, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its hardware-executed (IBM Heron r2) rare-disease-application finding remains summarized in this trend's history above
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41721 — Guo, Zheng, Qian, Zheng, Yang, Debbah, Yuen: "Leveraging Quantum Measurement Randomness for Shot-Efficient Diffusion Models" — turns quantum-measurement shot noise from a liability into a resource: characterizes the finite-shot multinomial sampling error of a QNN's linear readout and uses it to replace part of the artificial noise a diffusion sampler would otherwise inject, formulating the reverse process on a bounded zero-sum hyperplane mapped to the probability simplex so a compensation noise matches the mean/covariance of the shot-noise-free update — a 20th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; displaces Ning Ning's NS-RIS Hidden-Quantum-Markov-Model result (2608.06554, Aug-6, added 2026-08-13, the trend's longest-resident item at ~81 days) — no information lost, its first-HQMM-beats-classical-HMM benchmark finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-08-13 history below
  - 2026-10-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37116 — Płodzień: "Generative quantum extreme learning machines" — sidesteps barren-plateau-prone circuit training entirely: a random latent vector is encoded into a quantum state, evolved with a FIXED (untrained) unitary and measured, with only a linear classical output layer trained on the resulting features — a 19th independent group, a genuinely distinct generative-model-training mechanism (extreme-learning-machine readout instead of a trained circuit) from the trend's existing diffusion/flow-matching/QAOA-sampling strands (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31330 — Mahassen, Jastrzebski, Kyriienko: "Quantum Fokker-Planck solvers as trainable generative models" — parameterizes the potential of a Langevin process rather than using layered variational circuits with parameterized rotations; the associated Fokker-Planck equation is Hermitianized and solved via quantum singular value transformation (a linear combination of cosine operators), encoding the square root of the stationary density so sampling proceeds by computational-basis measurement; trains to match empirical multivariate distributions with demonstrated scalability — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 16 prior
  - 2026-10-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15083 — Zhang, Lee, Finke, Zhuang: "Generative learning of time series with holographic quantum circuit sampling" — holographic quantum circuit sampling (mid-circuit measurement + qubit reuse + independently-parameterized temporal blocks) proves inverse-polynomial (not exponential) loss-variance concentration bounds for Haar-random generators learning long time series, directly addressing the field's own growing-qubit/concentrated-loss-landscape problem — a 16th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged queue candidate held since 2026-10-13
  - 2026-10-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07196 — Wang, Tan, Gerstein: "Learning Disentangled Representations with Quantum Variational Autoencoders" — investigates how a QVAE's latent factors are organized/encoded, extending the disentangled-representation-learning framing from classical VAEs to quantum circuits for interpretable, controllable generation — a 15th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 14 prior
  - 2026-09-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31070 — Venturelli, Martina, Parigi, Caruso, Cervera-Lierta, González Ballester: "Quantum Diffusion Models for Medical Image Analysis" — a scalable hybrid Quantum Diffusion Model whose forward dynamics are modeled by a Discrete-Time Quantum Walk EXECUTED on a real quantum device, paired with a classical backward-denoising model, applied to medical image analysis — a 14th independent group (DORMANCY REVIVAL + CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 13 prior
  - 2026-09-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06307 — Hoseinpour Asli, Hashemian, Ardeshir-Larijani: "Hierarchical Fourier Approximation for Variational Quantum Distribution Learning" — a hierarchy of Walsh-Fourier approximations for training circuit Born machines, with an end-to-end expected-learning guarantee bounding the approximation term by the omitted Fourier mass — a 13th independent group, a rigorous theoretical learning guarantee complementing the trend's mostly-empirical architecture/application results
  - 2026-10-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35665 — Neto, Amaral, Temporão: "$\kappa$-Parametrized Boltzmann Distribution Sampling on Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm" — uses QAOA to sample the Boltzmann distribution needed to train a Restricted Boltzmann Machine, directly addressing RBM training's classical sampling intractability — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP)
notes: Promoted from observation_queue 2026-06-29 via convergence check — 3 independent groups (Wells Fargo/Chen group, Waller et al./Brunel, Banks et al.) on quantum generative and sequential learning within one week. Distinct sub-axis: using parameterized quantum circuits as generative/sequential models rather than discriminative classifiers. Covers diffusion-style generation, recurrent quantum circuits (QLSTM, fast-weight programmers), qudit generative models, and now flow-matching on the spin Wigner function (Hahm et al.). Stage promoted seed → emerging 2026-07-02: 4 independent groups within ~10 days (Jun 22 – Jul 1), clearing the "multi-source, forming" bar. Weekly recalibration (2026-07-04): held at `emerging`/`low` — 4 groups is right at the promotion threshold, not yet the 8-10-group breadth trend-001/002 showed before their `accelerating` promotion; confidence held at low pending another week of sustained corroboration (only 2 days old at `emerging`). 2026-07-09: Xanadu/PennyLane team's spectral Born machines (2607.06675) add a 5th independent group and, notably, the group's own reproducible tooling (a `tcdq` PennyLane module) plus a 190-qubit / 1M-parameter generative demo on real rRNA data — a step-change in scale for circuit-generative models. ### [id: trend-004] QML trainability: barren plateaus and noise-robustness theory

alias: qml-trainability-theory
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-01
last_evidence: 2026-11-12
evidence:
  - 2026-07-28 (v2 revision; originally posted 2026-05-12 — found via Quandela's own (undated) IEEE Quantum Week 2026 recap blog, accessed 2026-11-05, a genuine capture-leak catch) — https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11879 — Xie, Notton, Senellart: "Pre-Asymptotic Trainability in Photonic Variational Circuits under Postselection" — shows trainability in passive PHOTONIC variational circuits is governed by how postselection redistributes weight across the operator space's irreducible decomposition, not simple exponential mixing; some postselection configurations stay polynomial while dual-rail postselection moves toward exponential concentration at moderate sizes — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), awarded 2nd place in the QML Best Paper ranking at IEEE Quantum Week 2026; displaces Monbroussou/Thomas/Mhiri/Holmes/Kashefi's classical-simulation-and-model-concentration-in-passive-linear-optics paper (2607.24728, Jul-27, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its representation-theoretic barren-plateau↔classical-simulability tradeoff for bosonic/photonic optics remains summarized in this trend's history above; both papers sit on the SAME photonic-trainability sub-theme, so the micro-cluster's coverage is preserved, not narrowed
  - 2026-11-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05079 — Seksaria, Desu, Prabhakar: "Data informed Ansatz Selection for Variational Quantum Circuits" — three rules fixing ansatz/depth/entanglement choices directly from the data and gate set, BEFORE any training: a Fourier-coefficient-tensor SVD on a subset of qubits indicates whether entanglement is needed and bounds the error of omitting it, and quantum-Fisher-information-matrix rank growth gives a second data-informed signal — a pre-training diagnostic rather than another post-hoc trainability study — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep
  - 2026-10-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32571 — Monaco, Slim, Xue, Krücker, Borras: "Classical Pre-Training of Large Shallow Parametrized Quantum Circuits through Pauli Propagation and deployment on Quantum Hardware" — applies the Pauli propagation framework to a broad class of QML loss functions, minimizing an explicit parametric loss expression entirely on classical hardware, then deploys the classically-pretrained parameters on IBM's `ibm_berlin` for 64-qubit VQE and discrete generative-model benchmarks — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing candidate flagged since 2026-10-29 and clearing the 16-day watch flagged by this week's mandatory dormancy checklist
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21778 — Poppel, Bucher, Zorn, Baumann, Wölckert, Linnhoff-Popien: "Same Spectrum, Harder First-Order Training: Phase-Locking in Single- versus Multi-Qubit Re-Uploading Circuits" — shows a single-qubit re-uploading circuit's width-for-depth exchange is not free in first-order training, due to a "phase-locking" effect from all parameters moving the Bloch-sphere state along one complex direction — a 16th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing candidate held since 2026-10-15 and clearing the 16-day watch flagged by this week's mandatory dormancy checklist
  - 2026-10-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11261 — Fime, Samiha, Zaman, Pados, Sklivanitis, Shahid: "Q-Capsule: A Localized Capsule-Based Quantum Neural Architecture for Barren Plateau Mitigation" — register partitioning, local readout, sparse inter-capsule coupling and QFIM-guided adaptive depth growth together restrict each observable's dominant support to a small capsule, preserving useful gradient signal as circuits scale — a 15th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving a flagged candidate held since 2026-10-09
  - 2026-09-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38800 — Nguyen, Mukherjee, Phan (IBM): "Breaking the Shot-Noise Barrier: Cached Recycled Variance-Reduced Gradients for Quantum Optimization" — a center-point-caching variance-reduction scheme (CRVG) recycles quantum measurements to cut the O(p) spatial-variance penalty that cripples O(1) simultaneous-perturbation gradient estimators for deep VQAs — a 14th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving a flagged candidate held since 2026-09-30
  - 2026-09-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31066 — Luu, Luu, Pham, Truong: "Modeling quantum neural network gradient with reinforcement learning" — RLQ-Grad, a reinforcement-learning-based optimizer (a spectrally-normalized PPO agent) that learns to propose QNN parameter updates directly (conditioned on parameters, loss, accuracy and previous update) rather than differentiating through the circuit, sidestepping both the barren-plateau gradient-variance problem and the O(L·2^n) cost of exact gradient estimation — a 13th independent group (DORMANCY REVIVAL + CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 12 prior
  - 2026-09-01 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01364-2 — Nguyen, Kieferová (npj Quantum Information, DOI 10.1038/s41534-026-01364-2, opened this session): "Theoretical guarantees of variational quantum algorithm with guiding states" — a VQA that predicts ground-state properties across families of many-body problems (amortized-inference-style learnable solver), with rigorous convergence AND generalization guarantees derived from a QPE-guiding-state argument — an 11th independent group, DORMANCY REVIVAL, filling a gap this trend had flagged (rigorous VQA training/convergence guarantees, not just gradient-survival diagnostics)
  - 2026-09-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04462 — Alcântara, Morais, Chaves: "A Representation-Theoretic Framework for Characterizing Barren Plateaus" — extends the Lie-algebraic barren-plateau toolset via a representation-theoretic framework under a 2-design hypothesis that does not require the initial state/observable to belong to the circuit's dynamical Lie algebra — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a genuine theoretical generalization of the DLA-based toolset several of this trend's groups already use
  - 2026-11-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16365 — Faryad, Alomairy: "Training quantum neural networks one job at a time" — when a QNN is trained through a cloud queue, a variational algorithm pays per round trip and a round trip can carry many circuits, so the classical optimizer decides how much is learned PER JOB, not only per shot; compares optimizers given the same job/shot budget that differ only in how each job is filled — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep

### [id: trend-005] Neural Quantum States: classical neural-network ansätze for quantum many-body wavefunctions

alias: neural-quantum-states
stage: emerging
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-03
last_evidence: 2026-11-05
evidence:
  - 2026-11-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08151 — Jiang, Zhao, Kumar, Nigam, Rosenberg, Babbush, Baczewski, Huggins, Lee: "Machine learning quantum data to accelerate quantum Monte Carlo" — compresses quantum-processor measurement data (from a QPU-prepared trial wavefunction) into machine-learning models that efficiently supply the wavefunction overlaps QC-AFQMC needs, removing the measurement-processing bottleneck that has limited the approach's scalability — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 18 prior; Google Quantum AI co-authorship (Babbush, Huggins)
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30257 — Oyedemi, Czischek: "Scalable Neural Wave Functions via Information Mixing and Hierarchical Network Architectures" — addresses the long-range-correlation/compute tradeoff between RNN-based and Transformer-based NQS ansätze with an information-mixing hierarchical architecture aimed at capturing long-range correlations at lower computational cost than full self-attention — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 17 prior
  - 2026-10-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03463 — Qin, Qu, Hero: "Generalization of Transformer-Based Neural Quantum States via In-Context Learning" — a theoretical generalization analysis of Transformer-based NQS, grounding their recently-demonstrated empirical in-context-learning ability (adapting to new Hamiltonians from context without weight updates) — a 15th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 14 prior, the first THEORETICAL (not just architectural) treatment of this trend's transformer/attention strand
  - 2026-09-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37733 — Fu, Wei, Wang, Shang: "Foundation Neural-Network Quantum States for Molecular Potential Energy Surfaces in Second Quantization" — a geometry-conditioned foundation NQS: a single autoregressive model learns a family of ground states from sparse anchor geometries via orbital alignment and provides wavefunctions at untrained geometries without further optimization, reaching chemical accuracy for N2/CO/H4 and cutting per-geometry cost 986x — a 14th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 13 prior, extending the foundation-model-NQS strand (Hamilton-Zero, Foundation Neural Effective Hamiltonian) to molecular electronic structure
  - 2026-09-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06054 — Ding, Reiher: "Propagating sparsely supported states with time-dependent neural quantum states" — introduces interpolation sampling (drawing from a distribution interpolating between the wavefunction and its time derivative) to fix a support mismatch that stalls existing time-dependent NQS methods on sparsely-supported initial states — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the 11 prior groups
  - 2026-09-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07645 — Krinitsin, Rigo, Abedi, Schmitt: "Geometric inflation of deviations challenges neural quantum states in dynamics of quantum Ising models" — a controlled benchmark (1D quantum Ising quench dynamics) via supervised state reconstruction establishes substantially tighter empirical upper bounds on the parameter count NQS need than previously assumed, showing accurate NQS dynamics simulation is surprisingly hard even in regimes of limited physical complexity — an 11th independent group (DORMANCY REVIVAL + CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the 10 prior groups (Markus Schmitt's group, distinct from the 10th-group Zhang/Jiang/Luo and from Heightman et al.)
  - 2026-08-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14208 — Zhang, Jiang, Luo: "Foundation Neural Effective Hamiltonian for Strongly Correlated Quantum Materials" — projects a family of Hamiltonians onto a compact subspace spanned by Foundation-Neural-Quantum-State samples, addressing FNQS's accuracy loss near phase transitions and its per-target-coupling sampling cost — Di Luo's group, an 11th-arrival independent group (CAP-SWAP), extending the foundation-model-NQS strand this trend gained with Hamilton-Zero
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26544 — Huang, Ou, Ozolins: "Neural Network Backflow with Low-Rank Multi-Determinant Updates" — a variational framework combining a neural-network backflow transformation with a multi-determinant expansion using low-rank shifts, capturing non-local correlations and sign structure for strongly correlated fermions; on the 2D Hubbard model reaches energies within 0.45% of auxiliary-field QMC at half-filling and captures intertwined charge/spin-density stripes at 1/8 doping — a 13th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the 12 prior
  - 2026-10-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23815 — Perez Fadon, Tam, Shackleton: "Anyonic Neural Network Wavefunctions" — develops variational neural-network-wavefunction ansätze for continuum models of a non-interacting Abelian anyon gas, moving numerical studies (previously limited to exact diagonalization at N≤4 anyons) to larger system sizes — a 16th independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's first anyonic-statistics item; displaces Wah/Kunst/Hibat-Allah's COMPASS non-Hermitian/frustrated-states framework (2607.13790, Jul-15, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its finding remains summarized in this trend's creation history above
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18967 — Graham, Abouelkomsan, Fu (MIT): "Non-Abelian quantum Hall states at strong Landau level mixing: finding anyons with first-principles AI" — a self-attention neural-network ansatz ("MagNet") gives the first complete solution of the non-Abelian fractional-quantum-Hall problem including all electrons and Landau levels, confirming anti-Pfaffian topological order at strong Landau-level mixing — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a 2nd, independently-arriving anyonic-systems result the SAME week as the item above, a genuine convergence signal on this trend's newest sub-theme; displaces Zhou/Huang/Xiao Yuan/Cai's annealed-gradient-descent paper (2607.18865, Jul-21, the trend's next-oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its finding remains summarized in this trend's creation history above

### [id: trend-006] Quantum-advantage skepticism: dequantization, honest baselines, and NISQ-advantage refutations

alias: quantum-advantage-skepticism
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-07
last_evidence: 2026-11-10
evidence:
  - 2026-11-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08444 — Angrisani, Mhiri: "Polynomial-time classical simulation of boson and fermion sampling under thermal loss" — a common classical-simulation framework giving polynomial-time sampling at inverse-polynomial total-variation error for Fock-state boson sampling, Gaussian boson sampling with fixed uniform squeezing, and fermion sampling with magic inputs, for any fixed nonzero overall loss rate — a 25th independent group (CAP-SWAP), directly targeting the noise-robustness of sampling-based quantum-advantage claims
  - 2026-11-02 — https://doi.org/10.1103/kq3v-whcq — Shrikhande, Bachhar, Rodríguez-Jiménez, Valeev, Mayhall (Indiana University + Virginia Tech, PRX Quantum, opened this session via today's PRX RSS sweep): "Pauli Propagation Enables Fast Classical Simulation of Strongly Correlated Quantum Systems" — a variational double bracket flow (vDBF) algorithm repurposes Pauli propagation (a technique developed for CLASSICAL simulation of quantum circuits) to efficiently approximate ground-state energies for strongly correlated systems beyond 1D, where tensor-network/DMRG methods struggle — a 25th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a dequantization-flavored result distinct from the trend's existing Pauli-propagation group (Monaco/Slim/Xue/Krücker/Borras, trend-004 evidence, no author overlap); displaces Rodriguez-Grasa/Jerbi/Sanz/Sweke's surrogate-based-dequantization-of-QRL paper (2609.16266, Sep-14, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its kernel-based-surrogate extension from supervised learning to quantum reinforcement learning remains summarized in this trend's history above
  - 2026-10-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35126 — Hellstern, Schacht, Zaefferer: "Comparing classical and quantum QUBO solvers: optimality, trajectory diagnostics, and limits of solver selection" — benchmarks classical, tensor-network and fixed-schedule quantum-annealing QUBO solvers across 180+ instances; a general-purpose classical branch-and-cut solver matches or beats the quantum approach on the large majority of benchmarks — a 24th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-27 (across 9 dailies)
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42069 — Chakrabarti, Herman, Kim, Ozgul: "The Quartic Gap Between Quantum and Dequantized Algorithms for Linear Algebra Is Tight" — proves that the known quartic gap in query complexity (quantum $\widetilde O(ρ)$ vs. dequantized classical $\widetilde O(ρ^4)$, for QRAM-accessed linear systems at fixed condition number/accuracy) is TIGHT in the worst case up to a log factor, constructing dense linear systems for every condition number where sampling the dequantized solution genuinely requires the full $ρ^4$ queries — a rigorous complexity-theoretic result pinning down exactly how far dequantization can close a concrete QML-adjacent advantage claim — a 23rd independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; displaces Tindall/Mello/Fishman/Stoudenmire/Sels's Science tensor-network dequantization of D-Wave's Advantage2 dynamics (science.org/doi/10.1126/science.adx2728, added 2026-08-24, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its headline-refuting classical-simulation finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-08-24 history below
  - 2026-10-19 (capture-leak catch — found via today's community-pulse/digest sweep tracing a QCR pointer back through a stale recirculated press release, opened this session) — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26785 — de la Fuente, Majaniemi, van Nieuwenburg, Frohnert: "Correlated decoding loses the surface code's effective distance below threshold" — shows standard reweighting in correlated matching/clustering surface-code decoders (the technique underlying PyMatching v2.3.0 and Riverlane's proprietary LCD decoder) can cause the decoder to fail on size-(d-1)/2 fault configurations a distance-d code should correct, reducing error suppression deep below threshold under circuit-level noise; a regularized reweighting rule restores the nominal distance scaling — a 21st independent group (CAP-SWAP), a direct honest-baseline scrutiny of a production decoding technique used by Google-validated and industry decoders, no author overlap with any of the trend's 20 prior
  - 2026-10-07 (v2 revision; originally posted 2025-09-19, over a year pre-scaffold — found via today's dormancy-probe news search after trend-006 reached the 21-day silent-window line, opened this session) — https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16410 — Christophe Pere: "Does Data Complexity Predict Quantum Advantage? A framework, a pre-specified test, and an attribution of the measured edge" — a pre-specified, budget-matched honest-baseline test across 27 datasets: a composite data-complexity measure fails to predict the quantum edge (pooled R²=−0.32 vs. a pre-specified 0.4 threshold), and the apparent signal tracks classical-baseline weakness rather than a robust quantum contribution (R²=+0.13 vs. logistic regression but −0.94 vs. the best of six standard classical models) — a 20th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a genuine capture-leak catch resolving the trend's apparent silence
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41268 — Faryad: "Testing hardware quantum machine learning claims with a null circuit: fill prediction on Binance futures with 129 qubits of an IBM Heron processor" — a null-circuit protocol for auditing hardware-QML advantage claims: since a quantum processor is not a static function (its state drifts job-to-job and calibration-to-calibration), the test swaps in a circuit carrying no information about the input and checks whether a classical model trained on its outputs still "learns" — a 26th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-30 (across several dailies), opened this session
  - 2026-11-10 — https://doi.org/10.22331/q-2026-11-10-2268 — Anand, Chen, Cryan, Freifeld, Goldberg, Guo, Zhang (Quantum journal, peer-reviewed, opened this session via today's Quantum-journal RSS sweep): "Simulating Gaussian boson sampling on graphs in polynomial time" — shows a distribution related to Gaussian Boson Sampling on GRAPHS, the one most of GBS's graph-theoretic/molecular-similarity applications actually sample from, admits a polynomial-time classical sampler (plus a 2nd polynomial-time result for a related Boson-Sampling distribution) — meaning quantum algorithms give NO exponential speedup for these graphical GBS applications specifically — a fresh independent group (CAP-SWAP), directly on this radar's own classical-quantum-boundary axis; displaces Chen/A.&nbsp;A.&nbsp;Mele/F.&nbsp;A.&nbsp;Mele/Preskill's bosonic-Gaussian-state-learnability paper (2609.26705, Sep-22, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its learning-theoretic crossover finding remains summarized in this trend's history above
  - 2026-10-05 (v2 revision; originally posted 2026-09-30 — found via a 2026-10-19 Quantum Frontiers blog pointer, a genuine capture-leak catch, opened this session) — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40250 — Naghdi, Anschuetz: "An (almost) efficient classical algorithm for sampling from typical Gibbs states" — gives strong evidence against an exponential quantum advantage in Gibbs SAMPLING (not just computing local expectation values) for the quantum p-spin model, via a classical algorithmic-stochastic-localization algorithm sampling to any polynomially small total-variation error; Gibbs sampling directly underlies Boltzmann-machine-based quantum learning algorithms and quantum-supremacy proposals, making this a dequantization result squarely on this radar's own QML lead axis — a 22nd independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 21 prior
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14422 — Doriguello, Luongo, Verma: "The energetic cost of loading classical data in quantum computers" — quantum algorithms for ML/chemistry/cryptanalysis often assume coherent access to classical data at negligible physical cost; this treats data loading as a hardware process, quantifying the dynamic energetic cost of a QRAM query on a superconducting processor via a metric-noise-resource framework relating query fidelity/power to gate duration and drive strength — a 27th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a fresh angle (physical/energetic, not just structural) on the same data-loading-cost scrutiny as Faryad's "The Input Problem" (already summarized in this trend's W33 history above), opened this session via today's arXiv cross-list sweep

### [id: trend-007] QML generalization theory: bounds, phenomenology, and the reference-structure requirement

alias: qml-generalization-theory
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-11
last_evidence: 2026-11-10
evidence:
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13103 — Sahin, Tüysüz, Galvin, Wallis: "Which data samples matter when training a quantum model?" — establishes a quantum-compatible form of V-usable information, a data-centric metric quantifying how much a given quantum model can actually extract from a dataset, enabling direct data-centric (not just model-centric) comparisons of QML models — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep, squarely on this trend's own generalization/information-theoretic axis; DORMANCY REVIVAL (moved dormant → accelerating, its pre-dormancy stage), resolving the trend's dormancy with a genuine new-evidence catch, not a capture failure; displaces Zhang/Jia/Li/Dong's finite-noise-optimum paper (2608.24229, Aug-25, the trend's most recent dormancy-revival addition and, among the current 10, the least tightly coupled to the bound-vs-phenomenology core this trend tracks) — no information lost, its noise-order-purity-parameter finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-08-26 history below
  - 2026-10-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24426 — Ryu, Hur, Park: "Margin-Based Generalization for Quantum Machine Learning with Classical Data Encoding" — develops a margin-based generalization framework covering both fixed and trainable quantum data encodings; for fixed encodings the existing margin bound transfers unchanged, while for trainable encodings a two-scale covering argument adds an encoding-capacity term of order Õ(√(L/m)) — an 11th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 10 prior, resolving the trend's dormancy on a fresh generalization-bound result squarely on its own axis (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-09-22 — http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/8k5v-ddtw — Kwon, Lie, Jiang (PRX Quantum, peer-reviewed, opened this session via the day's PRX Quantum RSS sweep): "Universal Sample Complexity Bounds in Quantum Learning Theory via Fisher Information Matrix" — a general framework proving the sample complexity of quantum learning is governed by the inverse Fisher information matrix — a 10th independent group (CAP-SWAP), FILLING the evidence cap, Liang Jiang is a notable QEC/QML theorist already tracked elsewhere on this radar (trend-001), and the result directly sharpens the trend's own Fisher-effective-dimension (2607.06230) and measurement-correlation certification-cost (2609.14424, appended yesterday) strands with a peer-reviewed, general-purpose bound
  - 2026-09-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24666 — Anthony, Burov, Piro, Dal Peraro, Javerzac: "Experimental evidence of generalization in quantum machine learning in small-data regime" — trains quantum convolutional neural networks (whose generalization bounds scale with trainable-parameter count rather than dataset size, per Caro et al. 2022) and reports EXPERIMENTAL evidence that this bound-predicted generalization behavior actually holds in a small-data regime — a 9th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 8 prior, the trend's first primarily-experimental (rather than purely theoretical) corroboration of its own bound-vs-phenomenology question
  - 2026-09-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14424 — Sulimov, Lehmann: "Certification cost of quantum models: measurement correlation, not parameter count" — derives the circuit-execution budget (Theta(B p^2 V/(eps^2 G))) needed to certify a trained variational quantum model's empirical Fisher-information matrix to a stated relative error under coordinate-wise parameter shift, showing the true cost driver is measured readout variance/squared-gradient scale rather than raw parameter count — an 8th independent group, directly sharpening the trend's own founding PAC-Bayesian Fisher-effective-dimension result (2607.06230) with a concrete certification-cost bound; no author overlap with any of the 7 prior groups
  - 2026-08-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31117 — Raj, Mathur, Perdomo-Ortiz: "'Train classical, deploy quantum' requires rethinking generalization" — examines the common quantum-generative-model strategy of training on a classical computer and reserving the quantum device only for deployment-time sampling, showing this split changes what generalization even means/requires for the deployed quantum sampler — a 7th independent group, from a well-known QML group (Perdomo-Ortiz), directly on this trend's bound-vs-phenomenology question but for the train/deploy-split setting specifically
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14368 — D'Ambruoso, Gramegna, Pomarico, Magnifico, Lupo: "Invariance can prevent overfitting and shortcut learning in Quantum Neural Networks" — equivariant QNNs incorporate symmetries by design but the specific benefit is hard to isolate since equivariant circuits are usually compared against architectures that also differ in gates/structure/size; this isolates the role of invariance in a controlled 8-qubit-model comparison (identical encoding/structure/parameter count), finding it prevents overfitting and shortcut learning on an invariant image-classification task — an 11th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing queue candidate held since 2026-11-10, opened this session
  - 2026-07-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08350 — Pranjić, Roth, Tutschku: "Grokking and epoch-wise double descent in quantum neural networks": first empirical report of grokking (delayed memorization→generalization) and epoch-wise double descent in a QNN; late-stage generalization decays as the weight-norm grows unbounded, and a weak weight-norm regularizer permanently preserves the post-grokking gains
  - 2026-07-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21409 — Kempkes, Gil-Fuster, Bravo-Prieto, Ijaz, Wilms, Eisert, van Nieuwenburg, Dunjko (FU Berlin / Leiden et al.): "Cautious optimism for deep parameterized quantum circuits" — shows gradient-based PQCs can IMPROVE on unseen data as model size grows, exhibiting DOUBLE DESCENT (contra the "larger models generalize worse" view); underpinned analytically via add-one-in perturbation techniques + random-matrix spectral properties and confirmed numerically on re-uploading PQCs across datasets/training-set sizes — a 4th independent group, directly corroborating and extending the grokking/double-descent phenomenology of 2607.08350
  - 2026-07-28 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01330-y — Casas et al. (Barcelona Supercomputing Center / Universitat de Barcelona; npj Quantum Information, peer-reviewed): "The role of data-induced randomness in quantum machine learning classification tasks" — introduces the "class margin," a metric merging average data-embedding-induced randomness with classification margin, analytically connecting a data-embedding map's randomness to classification accuracy for binary classification — a 5th independent group, adding a peer-reviewed data-embedding-centric bound to the bound-vs-phenomenology strands

### [id: trend-008] Quantum reservoir computing: fixed quantum dynamics as a trainable-readout feature map

alias: quantum-reservoir-computing
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-13
last_evidence: 2026-11-04
evidence:
  - 2026-11-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07187 — Werlang, Valente: "Thermally-driven quantum reservoir computing" — encodes the reservoir's data injection directly on the time-dependent temperature of a thermal bath rather than a coherent drive, derives an exact nonlinear-response expression for any fixed readout, and proves a rigorous memory-nonlinearity trade-off that is a structural necessity of the thermal-encoding scheme rather than an engineering limitation — a 25th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a genuinely new encoding paradigm (thermal rather than coherent/drive-based injection)
  - 2026-11-03 — https://quantumcomputingreport.com/firstqfm-demonstrates-quantum-reservoir-computing-on-rigettis-108-qubit-processor-to-outperform-classical-financial-benchmarks/ — FirstQFM, in collaboration with SW7 Group and Rigetti (technical press coverage, opened this session): a blinded out-of-sample financial-forecasting pilot on Rigetti's 108-qubit Cepheus-1-108Q (61-84 physical qubits used for hardware-stabilized reservoir embeddings) outperforms a pooled MLP, PCR/PLS regression, asset-specific Random Forest and Amazon Chronos-2 zero-shot across directional accuracy, macro-F1, cumulative P&L (2.12x the top classical baseline) and Sharpe ratio (1.22, vs. ≤1.02 classical), across 43 anonymized assets and 600 out-of-sample trading dates, while explicitly disclaiming general quantum advantage — a 24th independent group (CAP-SWAP): FirstQFM already anchors trend-002's evidence (gate-time optimization), but this is its first appearance on the fixed-reservoir-dynamics axis specifically
  - 2026-10-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34055 — Laskar: "Quantum neuroplastic learning for time-series processing" — reorganizes the couplings among a quantum reservoir's qubits via eligibility traces (akin to synaptic plasticity) rather than keeping the reservoir dynamics fixed — a 23rd independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-27 (across 4 dailies); opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; displaces Sannia/Menta/Sathe/De Santis/Giovannetti/García-Pintos/Venturelli/Giorgi/Zambrini/Caravelli's 4,500-qubit quantum-annealer temporal-processing scale record (2609.19308, Sep-16, added 2026-09-16, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its real-hardware scale-record finding (largest QML experiment to date, reverse-annealing interactions proven indispensable) remains summarized in this trend's 2026-09-16 history below
  - 2026-10-19 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26228 — Baumann, Stein, Linnhoff-Popien: "The Fundamental Memory Ceiling of Noisy Quantum Reservoirs" — derives a rigorous universal upper bound on reservoir memory as a function of fresh-qubit reinitialization rate under depolarizing noise — a 21st independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing candidate held since 2026-10-20 and clearing the 14-day watch flagged by this week's mandatory dormancy checklist
  - 2026-10-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14454 — Nguyen, Soh: "Quantum-Fisher-information-aware optimization yields a quantum reservoir advantage over tuned classical reservoirs" — a QFI-optimized driven Jaynes-Cummings reservoir beats a tuned echo-state network at every size from 4 to 30 trained weights on Mackey-Glass forecasting, ahead of 99.5% of 10,000 random networks, after grid/Bayesian search alone already saturates — a 20th independent group, resolving the standing flagged cap-swap candidate held since 2026-10-10 (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11360 — Kar, Babu H: "When Does Interference Help Learning? Kernel Geometry as a Pre-Experimental Test for Photonic Reservoir Computing" — resolves conflicting reports of photonic-reservoir advantage/null-results/train-only-effects task-independently via the kernel geometry of boson-sampling feature maps, showing interference spreads feature variance across roughly twice as many effective dimensions as distinguishable-particle statistics — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-08, a pre-experimental DIAGNOSTIC (predicting whether interference will help BEFORE running a given task) rather than another application demo or theory paper
  - 2026-10-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05213 — Cao, Xia, Li: "Theory of the Edge-of-Chaos Advantage in Quantum Reservoir Computing" — develops an explicit quantum reservoir model and rigorously analyzes its chaotic vs. integrable regimes, showing Gaussian-unitary-ensemble statistics drive exponential memory loss in the chaotic regime — the first rigorous THEORY behind the edge-of-chaos performance heuristic this trend's own evidence had previously only tested empirically (Freiheit & Campaioli, displaced 2026-09-23) — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-04
  - 2026-09-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36901 — Sen, Parida, Chauhan, Arya, Bondar: "Digital Twin Modeling of Quantum Dynamical Systems: Dissipative Quantum Reservoir Computing" — a minimal single-qubit dissipative-QRC reservoir reproduces the high-harmonic-generation response of a much larger Ising spin chain, matching/surpassing prior temporal-convolutional and Kolmogorov-Arnold-network models while using a simpler physically-realizable system — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-09-29
  - 2026-09-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18694 — Selimović, Agresti, Siemaszko, Morris, Dakić, Albiero, Crespi, Ceccarelli, Osellame, Stobińska, Walther (PRX Quantum, DOI 10.1103/jknv-3tx7; orig. posted Apr-2025, published PRX Quantum Sep-25, opened this session via the day's PRX Quantum RSS sweep): "Experimental neuromorphic computing based on quantum memristor" — the first neuromorphic architecture based on a photonic quantum memristor, using a memristive feedback loop to supply the nonlinearity closed quantum evolution otherwise lacks, without entangling gates — a 15th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 14 prior, now peer-review-strengthened
  - 2026-10-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34430 — Wang, Ce: "Emergent Recursive Kernels in Quantum Reservoir Computing" — proves that a quantum reservoir with a fixed input-coupling-plus-Haar-unitary evolution admits an exact recursive kernel in the large-dimension limit, reducing each kernel evaluation to a scalar recurrence linear in history length and independent of reservoir size — a 22nd independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's most rigorous mechanistic derivation of WHY a fixed-dynamics QRC kernel behaves as it does
