### [id: trend-011] Quantum-advantage frontier: provable learning separations and honest quantum-classical crossovers

alias: quantum-advantage-provable-separations
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-25
last_evidence: 2026-11-10
evidence:
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41694 — Zhang, Shao: "Exponential quantum-classical query separations for cyclic Forrelation" — nearly completes the randomized classical query-complexity characterization of cyclic Forrelation (solvable with k quantum queries in the DQC1 model), resolving most of an open conjecture from FOCS'26 on the classical lower bound
  - 2026-10-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32595 — Kammerlander, O'Brien, King, Somma, Kieferová, Lackey, Schmidhuber, Jain, Gu, Alexeev, Chakrabarti, Shaydulin, Posta, Low, Gilboa, Troyer, Sanders, Christandl, Kyoseva, Lefebvre, Babbush (Open Quantum Institute/CERN + Google Quantum AI + Microsoft + JPMorganChase + MIT + Harvard et al., opened this session — a capture-leak catch, found via today's Quantum Computing Report/TQI-digest sweep, ~2 weeks uncaptured despite its multi-lab authorship): "The Compendium of Super-Quadratic Computational Quantum Advantages" — a structured, consensus enumeration of 24 computational problems with credible evidence for more-than-quadratic quantum speedup (spanning number theory, optimization, statistical inference, quantum simulation and linear algebra), characterizing the structural constraints real-world instances must satisfy to realize the advantage in practice and explicitly weighing data-loading/error-correction overhead against the full classical-vs-quantum comparison — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's first field-wide reference map (rather than a single result) and a direct, rigorously-scoped counterpart to trend-006's skepticism leg; displaces Kannan/Prabhu/Khan/Sohoni/Song/Roy/Senanian/Fatemi/McMahon/Cotler's single-qubit signal-learning paper (2608.13521, added 2026-08-14, by far the trend's longest-resident item at ~87 days) — no information lost, its hardware-demonstrated 10^7-fold measurement-reduction finding remains summarized in this trend's W33 history below. Also added to `study_shelf`.
  - 2026-11-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08180 — Hamoudi, Le Borgne, Teganahally Sridhara: "Quantum Advantage with Sign-Free Proofs: An Oracle Separation between StoqMA and MA" — shows a minimal quantum resource (no coherent Fourier-transform-based computation) already suffices to separate MA from StoqMA relative to an oracle, strengthening the FOCS/STOC lineage of MA⊆QCMA⊆QMA oracle separations with a much weaker quantum ingredient — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38922 — Goldar, Qin, Zhu, Gong, Wakin: "Adaptive Local Measurements for Quantum Learning Can Exponentially Outperform Nonadaptive Global Measurements" — shows an exponential learning advantage from measuring one copy at a time ADAPTIVELY, with no quantum memory needed across copies, in a setting where known exponential advantages otherwise required coherent measurements across copies or restricted-measurement assumptions — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a genuinely new provable-separation regime (adaptivity alone, not entanglement/memory, driving the exponential gap); displaces Arunachalam/Dutt/Krovi/Sengupta's "Separating quantum circuits from classical LLMs" (2608.03962, Aug-4, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its transformer/diffusion-LM-targeting separation remains summarized in this trend's creation history above
  - 2026-10-12 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01391-z — Coroi, Oh (npj Quantum Information, peer-reviewed, opened this session): "Exponential advantage in continuous-variable quantum state learning" — proves sample-complexity lower bounds for learning bosonic CV quantum states via their characteristic function, showing entanglement-free protocols cost exponentially many samples in general but a two-copy entangled-measurement-plus-reflected-state scheme achieves mode-independent sample complexity — a 15th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-13, extending the trend's provable-learning-separation strand to continuous-variable (bosonic) systems for the first time
  - 2026-10-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01902 + https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02145 — a 2-paper decoded-quantum-interferometry (DQI) advantage capture, TWO independent groups the same week: Wu & Yang's "Exponential quantum advantages for decoded quantum interferometry in the streaming setting" (an unconditional space-advantage proof in the one-pass streaming model) and Kramer/Gil-Fuster/Jones/Eisert/Schreiber's "A provable quantum advantage for approximate optimization via decoded quantum interferometry" (an oracle-separation proof on a folded optimal-polynomial-intersection task) — both extending Jordan et al.'s DQI algorithm (Nature 2025) with fresh provable-advantage results — a 13th AND 14th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-01
  - 2026-09-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40050 — Caha, Koenig, Paletta: "Unconditional quantum advantage with noisy planar architectures" — the first complexity-theoretic separation for 2D-local quantum circuits under constant-threshold local stochastic noise that is simultaneously unconditional, noise-resilient and geometrically planar-constrained, beating noise-free unbounded-fan-in AC0 classical circuits — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-09-30
  - 2026-11-10 — https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01370-4 — Pirnay, Jerbi, Seifert, Eisert (npj Quantum Information, peer-reviewed, opened this session via today's npj QI research-articles sweep): "An unconditional distribution learning advantage with shallow quantum circuits" — proves an unconditional PAC distribution-learning separation for constant-depth quantum circuits (one/two-qubit gates) over constant-depth bounded-fan-in/fan-out classical circuits, technically uplifting Bene Watts & Parham's shallow-circuit SAMPLING advantage (already tracked on this trend) into a hyperplane-learning problem and identifying non-local correlations as the advantage's origin — a fresh independent group (CAP-SWAP), directly on this radar's own QML lead-axis/provable-separation theme; displaces Lee/Shin/Lee's two-round-CHSH unconditional-advantage paper (2609.21237, Sep-18, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its finding remains summarized in this trend's history above
  - 2026-10-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23268 — Oliveira: "Unconditional Quantum PAC Learning Advantage in the Large Error Regime" — establishes an unconditional super-polynomial separation between quantum and classical PAC learning of Boolean functions under the uniform distribution in the large-error regime: for every advantage λ(n)=n^{-ω(1)}, a polynomial-time quantum learner using only quantum random examples learns to agreement 1/2+λ(n) with high success probability, provably impossible for any efficient classical learner — a 16th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a clean complexity-theoretic (not hardware-claimed) separation squarely on this trend's provable-learning-separation theme; displaces Luo/Liu/Allcock/Zhang/Lui's stochastic-optimization speedup (2607.25492, Jul-28, the trend's oldest remaining item, an SGD-primitive speedup rather than a learning-separation result) — no information lost, its finding remains summarized in this trend's creation history above
  - 2026-10-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17648 — Ye: "Nearly Optimal Quantum Sample Complexity of Learning Fourier-Sparse Boolean Functions" — gives a near-optimal Õ(k) quantum-sample-complexity learner for k-Fourier-sparse Boolean functions using only separate (non-collective) measurements, improving the previous O(k^1.5 log²k) bound — a 16th independent group (CAP-SWAP)

### [id: trend-012] AI-for-quantum (circuit-synthesis leg): generative/transformer models that directly synthesize quantum circuits

alias: ai-for-quantum-circuit-synthesis
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-31
last_evidence: 2026-10-30
evidence:
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41957 — Loharkar, Castañeda Medina: "GENIE: learned quantum circuits as embedding impurity solvers, and why compactness, not correlation strength, decides when they work" — GENIE, a DMET impurity solver in which a generative transformer LEARNS the sampling circuit for sample-based quantum diagonalization (trained on the quantum-selected configuration-interaction energy), compared at equal determinant budget against classical selected-CI methods and fixed-circuit SQD on N2, hydrogen chains, water and a hydrogen ring — a 14th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep, directly matching this trend's "generative/transformer models that directly synthesize quantum circuits" framing for a chemistry-impurity-solver circuit rather than an algorithm/QEC-code circuit; displaces Huidobro-Meezs/Paiva-Ortega/Vargas-Hernández's Grinch generative-optical-experiment-design paper (2609.08073, Sep-8, added 2026-09-09, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its reward-driven generative-framework-for-photonic-experiment-design finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-09-09 history below
  - 2026-10-27 — https://doi.org/10.1088/2058-9565/aeb3b0 — Over, Bengoechea, Borello Busilacchi, Kiffner, Rung, Michailidis (Quantum Science and Technology, published Oct-27): "Operator learning for efficient quantum computation" — maps discrete operators onto compact, structured variational quantum circuits tailored to target-hardware connectivity, trained via backpropagation with a cost function that optimizes unitary AND non-unitary (dense/sparse) operators using only a single ancilla qubit for block encoding, plus a regularization term reducing approximation error
  - 2026-10-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29779 — Petronilo, Araújo, Teixeira, Cruz: "Mamba Quantum Eigensolver (MQE): A Selective State Space Model for Scalable Ground State Simulations in Quantum Chemistry" — replaces the Transformer backbone of the Generative Quantum Eigensolver (which reformulates ground-state search as discrete gate/ansatz-sequence generation) with a Mamba selective-state-space model, trading the Transformer's quadratic attention cost for Mamba's linear-time sequence modeling to scale GQE-style circuit generation to larger molecules — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's first architectural swap away from Transformer-based sequence generation
  - 2026-10-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25213 — Peled, Nachmani: "LiftDiff: Guided Discrete Diffusion for Quantum Code Construction" — a discrete denoising diffusion model, trained to generate base matrices over the group algebra F2[Z_ℓ], is expanded into lifted-product qLDPC codes; the reverse diffusion process is guided by an energy function evaluating quantum-code properties — an 11th independent group, extending the trend's generative-synthesis pattern from circuits/magic-state factories/BB-codes to a DIFFUSION-model-specific QEC-code-discovery method (CAP-SWAP)
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22367 — Gu, Kemeny, Yelin, Lukin (Harvard): "Discovering state-of-the-art quantum circuits with large language models" — an evolutionary framework where LLMs propose quantum-circuit designs for computational primitives (optimizing the resource costs that dominate practical quantum algorithms under quantum computing's reversibility constraint, absent from ordinary classical computation), iterating via evaluation feedback — a 10th independent group, FILLING the evidence cap, and the trend's first item from a leading quantum-hardware experimentalist's group (Mikhail Lukin) rather than a software/ML-methods team
  - 2026-10-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06535 — Jain, Wills, Singh: "Symmetry and AI-assisted discovery of magic-state factories" — develops symmetry- and AI-assisted search methods (a unified binary-matrix formulation spanning triorthogonal-code and direct circuit searches, with group symmetry and LANGUAGE-MODEL AGENTS restricting the syndrome search, followed by deterministic solving and independent verification) to directly synthesize magic-state-factory circuits at distance ≥3, a regime previously intractable at moderate input counts; yields 699 verified factories — a 9th independent group, extending the trend's generative/AI-circuit-synthesis pattern to FTQC resource-state factories specifically
  - 2026-10-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06623 — Cruz-Benito: "Large Language Model-Guided Discovery of Weight-Five Bivariate Bicycle Codes" — continuing an LLM-guided program-evolution workflow, searches weight-five bivariate/perturbed-bivariate-bicycle QEC codes, producing a catalogue of 1,142 distinct code proposals (1,081 attributable to LLM-generated programs) including three newly-certified CSS code parameters and a basis-independent connectivity analysis — an 8th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 7 prior, extending the trend's AI-circuit/code-synthesis pattern to QEC code-family discovery
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25947 — Hofstetter, Yeh, Murali: "Bridge of $Ψ$'s: Quantum Circuit Optimization with Schrödinger Bridges" (BOPS) — a generative model based on Schrödinger bridges with a custom denoiser architecture learns a transformation from a source circuit into an equivalent, optimized one, trained on data constructed to be hard for existing optimizers (rewrite rules applied backwards); on held-out 8-qubit x 64-depth Clifford+T circuits reduces gate count 2.46x and depth 2.45x (geometric mean), outperforming all nine baseline optimizers — a 7th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 6 prior, described by its own authors as the first generative model bridging quantum circuits and frontier ML methods
  - 2026-09-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24657 — Wang, Xiong, Dai, Liu: "Circuit Hypernetworks for Quantum-Augmented Diffusion Language Models" — HyperQ adds token-conditioned quantum residual branches to a frozen masked-diffusion language model: a hypernetwork reads a token's hidden state and emits the coordinates of that token's quantum circuit, which is executed and added back through a residual connection, keeping the backbone frozen — a 6th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 5 prior, generalizing the trend's generative-circuit-synthesis pattern from standalone algorithm/experiment circuits to per-token circuits inside a large frozen language model
  - 2026-09-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11248 — Kundu, Jaiswal, Feld, Tiwari: "Generative Replay Mitigates Sample Starvation in Quantum Architecture Search" — GenQAS, a tensor-network-guided RL framework combining a fixed matrix-product-state warm-start with prioritized GENERATIVE replay: a learned local transition model synthesizes additional predicted circuit transitions on demand to fix sample starvation in RL-driven quantum architecture search — a 5th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 4 prior, extending the trend's generative-circuit-synthesis pattern to RL-based architecture search specifically

### [id: trend-013] Agentic AI directly operating quantum hardware and lab infrastructure end-to-end

alias: agentic-ai-quantum-lab-automation
stage: emerging
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-08-01
last_evidence: 2026-10-29
evidence:
  - 2026-10-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40589 — Mansouri, Kimura, Koga, Arai: "Collaborative AI agents for autonomous experimental research with solid-state quantum spins" — multiple LLM-based agents assess one another's work and can stop/redirect investigations (generating and rejecting research questions, requiring separate theoretical checks, designing experiments to challenge their own claims), demonstrated end-to-end on real ensembles of NV-diamond quantum spins (deriving and computationally verifying the minimum calibration settings needed to predict microwave-pulse-sequence responses) — a 10th independent group, no author/org overlap with any of the trend's 9 prior, and the first evidence item whose novelty is the multi-agent adversarial self-review process itself rather than a new hardware platform; cap reached (10/10)
  - 2026-10-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25014 — Sheng, Xiao, Zhang, Wang, Zhang, Geng, Huang, Li: "Large Language Model Steering of Acousto-Optic Tweezer Arrays" — a general-purpose LLM acts as a human-in-the-loop strategy layer for a neutral-atom tweezer platform, guiding a transition from multiplicative row-column correction to a drift-corrected local-response-matrix method when separable feedback stalls, on a real 10x10 crossed-acousto-optic-deflector array (deterministic software performs image analysis/numerical updates; an experimentalist verifies/authorizes each waveform) — a 9th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 8 prior, arriving just 3 days after the 8th
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21047 — Hou, Wu, Zhang, Feng, Li, Xiang: "Agent-assisted experimental strategy transfer across quantum processors" — an agent assesses experimental evidence, selects subsequent measurements and adapts procedures to transfer an experimental strategy developed on one three-qubit NMR quantum processor sequentially to four other NMR processors, each with device differences and drift that would otherwise make the original workflow unsuitable — a 7th independent group, no author/org overlap with any of the trend's 6 prior (Pasqal; Kreppel et al.; the NV-sensing group; QuEra; OpenAI; Reed/Daly/Beling/Giandrea), and the first evidence item on this trend from an NMR quantum-computing platform rather than superconducting/neutral-atom/trapped-ion hardware
  - 2026-10-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17193 — Reed, Daly, Beling, Giandrea: "Principles and Techniques for Agentic Research in Experimental Physics" — outlines ten requirements for human-centered collaborative AI agents in experimental physics and a framework enforcing them, demonstrated across six closed-loop case studies including a quantum-control pulse sequence candidate improving simulated performance by more than an order of magnitude and a solid-state quantum-defect photodynamic model substantially outperforming the authors' own published work — a 6th independent group, no author/org overlap with any of the trend's 5 prior (Pasqal, Kreppel et al., the NV-sensing group, QuEra, OpenAI), and the first evidence item framing the pattern as a general, principles-based methodology for agentic lab automation rather than a single-system demonstration
  - 2026-10-08 — https://www.pasqal.com/blog/turning-ideas-into-qpu-experiments-how-agentic-workflows-make-quantum-computing-more-accessible/ — Pasqal (technical blog, opened this session; publicizing arXiv:2607.25834, the trend's own founding evidence item, now shipped as a live product): launches the "Neutral-Atom Agentic Toolkit," a plugin for Claude Code/Cursor/Codex that chains dedicated skills (application-to-idea, idea-to-spec, spec-to-sequence, validate-emu/noise-emulate, qpu-submit, harvest-and-analyze) to take a research idea, paper or patent all the way to a validated, executed and analyzed experiment on one of three real Pasqal Cloud QPUs (Ruby/GENCI, FC1/Sherbrooke, SA1/Saudi Arabia); the post itself walks through the paper's 3 test cases and is explicit that "execution success is not the same as scientific correctness" (the theory-to-hardware case needed a domain expert to redirect an incomplete observable choice) — NOT a new independent group (same org, Pasqal, as the trend's founding item), but a dormancy-reviving, significant maturation from a research prototype to a shipped, publicly-documented product
  - 2026-09-08 — https://openai.com/index/codex-quantum-computing-experiments/ — OpenAI (technical blog, opened this session): "How GPT-5.6 Sol helps run quantum computing experiments" — Beatriz Yankelevich (MIT Engineering Quantum Systems Group) connects GPT-5.6 Sol, harnessed to Codex, directly to the lab software controlling a six-qubit superconducting chip's dilution refrigerator and control electronics; the agent autonomously selects measurement settings, runs calibration sequences, analyzes returned signals and decides what to measure next, identifying qubit resonance frequencies, calibrating control/readout pulses and measuring coherence times with limited intervention on clear signals (more researcher help needed on weak/noisy ones) — a 5th independent group, distinct from all 4 prior (Pasqal; Kreppel et al.; the NV-sensing group; QuEra), and the first evidence item on this trend from a general-purpose AI lab (OpenAI) partnering with an academic group rather than a quantum-hardware vendor or a quantum-focused research team

  - 2026-07-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25834 — Dalyac, Dauphin, Henriet, Jurczak (Pasqal): "Lowering the implementation barrier of neutral-atom quantum computing with agentic workflows" — an LLM agentic workflow autonomously runs the full protocol-design → compilation → simulation → cloud-execution pipeline on two REAL Pasqal QPUs overnight from a published paper/patent, with the researcher kept in the loop for validation; honestly reports two agent failure modes (an inadequate observable choice; a plausible-but-incorrect hardware diagnosis) caught only by domain-expert review, not by the agent itself
  - 2026-07-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24714 — Kreppel, Salkhordeh, Schmidt-Kaler, Brinkmann: "Efficient LLM-Generated Shuttling Compilers for Complex Trapped-Ion Architectures" — a single frontier LLM (Claude Opus 4.7) autonomously generates and iteratively refines the full Python code of ion-shuttling compilers from written specifications, progressing from a linear segmented trap to a trap with junctions — an LLM writing (not just tuning) real quantum-hardware compiler infrastructure
  - 2026-07-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25145 — "Agentic AI for Scientific Reasoning in Autonomous Quantum Sensing Experiments" — an LLM agent autonomously runs NV-diamond quantum-sensing experiments end-to-end (calibration, T2* measurement); watch-area-adjacent (quantum sensing, not quantum computing) but the same agentic-lab-automation pattern as the other two evidence items

### [id: trend-014] Learning Hamiltonian and dissipative-rate parameters of quantum systems from data

alias: quantum-hamiltonian-parameter-learning
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-09-01
last_evidence: 2026-11-11
evidence:
  - 2026-11-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07353 — Yang, Liu, Ding, Yan: "Observable-Based Learning of Effective Entanglement Hamiltonians" — develops an observable-matching framework for learning effective entanglement Hamiltonians from quantum-Monte-Carlo response estimates, calibrating the lattice Bisognano-Wichmann construction's overall prefactor and then learning spatially-resolved couplings beyond its prescribed profile without assuming a specific functional form — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the trend's approach to dormancy with a genuine new-evidence catch rather than a capture failure
  - 2026-10-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06709 — Gong, Ma, Chen, Cotler, Huang: "Out-of-control Hamiltonian Learning" — studies Hamiltonian learning under MINIMAL quantum-control access models motivated by analog atom/ion platforms (uniform state preparation + fixed-basis measurement only, no fast arbitrary single-qubit gates); shows all parameters of a generic 2-local Hamiltonian on any interaction graph are surprisingly still reconstructible from such restricted experiments — a genuine new independent group (Jordan Cotler, Hsin-Yuan Huang) directly addressing the near-term-hardware-realism gap in every prior learning protocol this trend has tracked, which largely assumed fuller quantum control
  - 2026-09-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39908 — Koor, Rebentrost: "Quantum Sensing and Hamiltonian Learning under Stochastic Parameter Evolution" — introduces stochastically evolving Hamiltonians (SEHs, local Hamiltonians whose coefficients are governed by stochastic differential equations), reanalyses and extends the Huang-Tong-Fang-Su Heisenberg-limit Hamiltonian-coefficient-learning protocol to this setting, and delineates where the Heisenberg limit can and cannot be retained — a 10th independent group, FILLING the evidence cap, no author overlap with any of the trend's 9 prior
  - 2026-09-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34995 — Zou, Kockum, Rahm, Olsson: "Simulation-Based Quantum System Inference with Neural Posterior Estimation" — a unified, likelihood-free framework pairing polynomial-cost classical simulators (Pauli propagation, tensor networks) with normalizing flows/other neural density estimators to learn Hamiltonian-parameter posteriors directly from classical simulation data, sidestepping the intractable-likelihood problem of the exponentially large Hilbert space — a 9th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 8 prior
  - 2026-09-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28391 — Dong, Leng: "Quantum score matching with applications to learning thermal states" — establishes a general quantum score-matching framework (extending classical score matching's normalization-constant-free training to noncommuting density operators) with efficient circuit implementations and theoretical guarantees, validated on learning thermal states — an 8th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 7 prior; notable as a SECOND, independent group converging on quantum score-matching for Hamiltonian/thermal-state learning within days of Shukla/Jayakumar/Lokhov's Operator Score Matching (2609.25246, appended yesterday) — the same method arriving from two unconnected teams almost simultaneously
  - 2026-09-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27629 — Xiu-Hao Deng: "Structured Hamiltonian Learning for Multiqubit Conditional Phase Gates" — casts diagnosing whether a measured conditional-phase gate matches its declared generalized-gate equivalence class as structured Hamiltonian learning of the diagonal successful block (Ramsey measurements on a connected graph plus a Walsh-transform eigenphase reconstruction), deriving a branch-independent equivalence criterion from multiplicative Boolean-Möbius invariants — a 7th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 6 prior
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26596 — Shin, Tong: "Hamiltonian Learning and Certification via Eigenphase Engineering" — a technique (eigenphase engineering) achieving Heisenberg-limited learning of k-local Hamiltonians using only forward time evolution at integer multiples of a fixed interval, with a near-maximal step size; recovers Pauli coefficients of an s-sparse Hamiltonian in total evolution time close to optimal, and also enables tolerant Hamiltonian certification and single-Pauli-coefficient estimation without locality/sparsity assumptions — a 5th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 4 prior
  - 2026-09-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25246 — Shukla, Jayakumar, Lokhov: "Operator Score Matching for Learning Quantum Hamiltonians" — a quantum analog of classical score matching, exploiting generalized derivatives/integration-by-parts in operator algebras, recovers Hamiltonian parameters from low-temperature thermal-state measurements via simple gradient descent, with no intractable partition-function computation; validated on the transverse-field Ising model, XXZ chain, Fermi-Hubbard model and lattice φ⁴ theory — a 6th independent group, no author overlap with any of the 5 prior
  - 2026-11-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15859 — Paul, Schuster, Alicea: "Learning Quantum Hamiltonians from the Dynamical Structure Factor" — studies learning a quantum Hamiltonian from the dynamical structure factor (DSF), which captures two-point dynamical correlations as measured via neutron scattering or quantum-simulator probes — a 13th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 12 prior, opened this session via today's arXiv sweep
  - 2026-10-16 — https://doi.org/10.1103/kjvf-jgqj — Shin, Lee, Oh (KAIST/Seoul National University, PRX Quantum, peer-reviewed, opened this session): "Heisenberg-Limited Hamiltonian Learning Without Short-Time Control" — shows Heisenberg-limited Hamiltonian learning is achievable without arbitrarily short-time control (every query has duration at least a prescribed minimum time T), removing the short-time-control dependence that limited near-optimal prior protocols and posed severe experimental challenges from finite control bandwidth and transient pulse errors — an 11th independent group (CAP-SWAP), a peer-reviewed relaxation of a standing assumption across this trend's own learning protocols; displaces Johnson/Dumitrescu's tensor-network Hamiltonian-learning-at-scale emulation (2608.23707, Aug-24, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its finding remains summarized in this trend's creation history above

### [id: trend-015] Quantum kernels & feature maps: expressivity, encoding budgets and application-scale benchmarking

alias: quantum-kernels-feature-maps
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-09-02
last_evidence: 2026-11-10
evidence:
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14179 — Giraldo, Ruiz, Caruso, Mohammadbagherpoor: "Quantum-Enhanced QSAR for DYRK1A: Evaluating Complementary and Utility QPU-Derived Molecular Representations" — compares tabular molecular descriptors against QPU-measured feature vectors (same RBF-SVM) for a kinase-inhibitor hit-identification task
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42029 — Bhalgami: "Empirical Evaluation of Quantum Kernel Methods for Classification: A Comparative Study with Classical Kernels" — a protocol-controlled quantum-vs-classical-kernel benchmark (shared bandwidth/hyperparameter search budget, per-fold recomputation, hardware-circuit-evaluation cost accounting) addressing why published quantum-kernel benchmarks disagree — a 21st independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolved from `observation_queue` (queued since 2026-11-02, flagged as a standing cap-swap candidate across the 11-02/11-03/11-04/11-10 dailies); displaces Repetto/Melchionna/D'Aurizio's IQP-feature-map RKHS-expressivity paper (2610.23692, Oct-16, added 2026-10-16/17, the trend's longest-resident remaining item) — no information lost, its RKHS-structure expressivity finding remains summarized in this trend's creation history below

Sep-2025 as arXiv:2509.18530) — Cha, Park, Lee: "Re-uploading quantum data: a universal function approximator for quantum inputs" — extends classical-input data re-uploading's universal-approximation result to QUANTUM inputs: a qubit interacting sequentially with fresh copies of an arbitrary input state, alternating entangling unitaries with mid-circuit resets, approximates any bounded continuous function using only one ancilla qubit and single-qubit measurements — a 19th independent group (CAP-SWAP), resolving the standing flagged candidate held since 2026-10-30
  - 2026-10-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40840 — Zhang, Culpitt, Yin, Zhu, Li: "Electronic-Structure-Informed Many-Body Encoding for Quantum Machine Learning" — a physics-informed quantum encoding exploiting the many-body structure of electronic systems, mapping n-body reduced-density-matrix descriptors to n-qubit gates for a systematic hierarchy of increasingly expressive quantum circuits; benchmarked on predicting electron-correlation energies along bond-stretching potential-energy surfaces, showing accuracy improves systematically with encoding body-order toward the full-CI limit — an 18th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; displaces Roh/Ahn/Lee/Park/Yoon/Aggarwal/Kim's SQUARE frozen-LLM quadratic-feature-map paper (2609.37134, Sep-29, added 2026-09-30, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its frozen-classical-LLM-adaptation finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-09-30 history below
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41848 — Payares, Milazzo, Ben Slama, Cope: "Engineered Learning Problems on Noisy Quantum Processors" — demonstrates a significant performance gap for a projected quantum kernel over classical methods at n=15, 30 and 45 qubits on an engineered classification problem, reproducible across different quantum processor units, and shows error mitigation widens this gap both by improving hardware performance and by raising the classical difficulty — a 17th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep; the authors explicitly caveat this is an ARTIFICIAL problem, not a real-world task, keeping it application-scale-benchmarking evidence rather than an advantage claim; displaces Wang/Roy/Akbari/Maskey/Ramachandran's structured-quantum-kernels-for-chaotic-forecasting paper (2609.13360, Sep-11, added 2026-09-15, the trend's longest-resident item) — no information lost, its encoding-structure-as-inductive-bias finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-09-15 history below
  - 2026-10-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39350 — Anai, Nakayama, Ooka, Tomoda, Morohoshi, Chin, Miki, Yabuno, Terai, van Loock, Mitarai, Takeda: "Provably efficient experimental classification of quantum data with quantum kernels" — establishes a theoretical framework linking quantum-state-classification efficiency to the structure of the quantum data itself (not a per-task physical description), with sufficient conditions for polynomial-measurement-count accurate classification; experimentally demonstrated on a programmable photonic processor, applied to classifying stellar rank (non-Gaussianity) in continuous-variable quantum computing — a 16th independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's first real-hardware photonic demonstration tied to a general theoretical guarantee rather than a numerics-only or gate-based result; displaces Finkelstein/Legziel Levy/Yakhini/Cohen's IQP-vs-Kernel-PCA credit-default study (2609.10505, Sep-9, the trend's oldest remaining item) — no information lost, its classical-non-linear-baseline-comparison finding remains summarized in this trend's creation history above
  - 2026-10-26 — https://doi.org/10.1088/2058-9565/ae5fc7 — Alonso-Linaje, Azad, Soni, Smalley, Lapworth, Arrazola (Xanadu/PennyLane + Rolls-Royce, Quantum Science and Technology, published Oct-26): "Quantum compilation framework for data loading" — an automated compilation framework for resource-aware quantum data loading that partitions a total error budget between exact and approximate state preparation per a target vector/error tolerance, supporting multiplexer-based loaders, QROM constructions, sparse encodings, matrix product states, Fourier-series loaders and Walsh-transform diagonal operators
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31260 — Jha, Lin, Rosnes: "Can Entanglement-Induced Feature-Map Designs Improve Projected Quantum Kernels?" — isolates the native inductive bias of projected quantum kernels (PQKs) by comparing untuned vs. tuned PQKs against an RBF baseline, exposing how heavily prior PQK studies' apparent performance rests on extensive classical hyperparameter tuning rather than the quantum feature map itself; proposes new two-qubit-entanglement feature maps targeting multivariate IoT-data correlations — a 14th independent group (CAP-SWAP), no author overlap with any of the trend's 13 prior
  - 2026-10-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29186 — Park, Jeong, Shim, Yoon: "Readout Dependent Parameter Identifiability of Quantum Kernels" — characterizes how a circuit's readout/measurement choice (metric design vs. nonlinear feature expansion) determines whether distinct circuit parameters remain distinguishable in the resulting projected-kernel Gram matrix, with an exact four-qubit family showing a sharp rank-two/dense-metric boundary — a 13th independent group (CAP-SWAP), directly on this trend's own expressivity/identifiability-theory strand

(Pasqal): "Benchmarking a machine-learning differential equations solver on a neutral-atom logical processor" — compares a quantum-kernel-method differential-equation solver run at the PHYSICAL vs. LOGICAL level on a real atom-based logical processor, showing the logical implementation outperforms its physical counterpart end-to-end (not just on isolated sub-routines), with noise-induced errors traced to specific sources the chosen encoding detects — a 20th independent group (CAP-SWAP), the trend's first item demonstrating QEC/logical encoding improving a quantum-kernel ML application specifically rather than a bare physical-qubit demo; displaces Micah/Milazzo/Beji/Aizpurua/Espinosa-Portalés/Payares/Ben Slama/Andrea/Kurek/Cope/Salomon's Pareto-optimal-kernel malware-beaconing paper (2610.09717, Oct-7, added 2026-10-08, the trend's longest-resident remaining item) — no information lost, its real-world-cybersecurity Pareto-optimization finding remains summarized in this trend's 2026-10-08 history above

### [id: trend-016] Quantum transformers and attention: PQC-based attention mechanisms for sequence and signal processing

alias: quantum-transformers-attention
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-09-17
last_evidence: 2026-11-12
evidence:
  - 2026-11-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05355 — Tongloy, Pongsopa, Jomtarak: "Reading Why: Born-Measured Attention with Exact Word Evidence on Light and Qubits" — a transformer where every step is a Born measurement (attention as |⟨q|k⟩|⁴ normalised per row, a measured product replacing the feed-forward nonlinearity, and a Born readout); with denominators held at their input values, each class probability becomes a polynomial in the word amplitudes, giving exact (not approximate) integrated-gradient word-evidence via finite quadrature — a 12th independent group (CAP-SWAP), opened this session via today's arXiv sweep
  - 2026-10-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17351 — Bhardwaj, Spannowsky: "QGrAT: Quantum Grade-Aware Transformer for Jet Tagging" — a quantum transformer for high-energy-physics jet tagging whose attention couplings are determined by grade-specific inner products of Clifford-algebra features built from constituent four-momenta, embedding Lorentz symmetry and hadronic-top three-prong structure directly into the attention mechanism — a 10th independent group, FILLING the evidence cap, no author overlap with any of the trend's 9 prior, and this trend's first entrant from particle-physics phenomenology rather than NLP/vision/chemistry/signal-processing applications
  - 2026-10-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04588 — Lin, Hsu, Li, Chen, Samuel Yen-Chi Chen: "Variational Quantum Attention for Molecular Graph Learning" (QGAT) — an edge-aware variational quantum attention mechanism for molecular graph learning in which the receiving atom, neighboring atom and connecting bond jointly determine the quantum attention state, achieving competitive performance against GATv2 across five molecular property/bioactivity tasks with a consistent BBBP improvement across six circuit ansätze, and a higher Spearman correlation than its classical counterpart on the Verubecestat BACE1 inhibitor series — a 9th independent-artifact entry (Samuel Yen-Chi Chen already anchors this radar's trend-003 evidence, so not a fully independent group radar-wide, but a genuinely new application domain — molecular graphs — for this trend's own attention-mechanism architectures)
  - 2026-10-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02641 — Friedewald, Alla, Shiri Sichani, Shyu: "When Normalization Selects the Sign: Auditing Robustness Ablations in Quantum Attention" — shows that removing an input-scaling module from a four-qubit quantum-attention detector changes both the classifier AND the perturbations reaching its encoder, so a robustness-ablation result can reflect the comparison rule rather than the module being tested, unless the attack budget is matched at the encoder's input rather than the raw physical budget — an 8th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 7 prior, the first honest-ablation-methodology contribution on this trend's own architectures (paralleling trend-006's QASA/QEMScore strand but applied to quantum attention specifically)
  - 2026-09-29 (v-revision surfaced this session via today's cs.ET cross-list feed; v1 originally posted 2026-04-29) — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00045 — Chung, Talebpour: "Universal Quantum Transformer" (UQT) — a quantum-native computing architecture using the physical properties of multi-qubit systems, aimed at classical continuous-space neural networks' struggle to lock into exact discrete/formal rules (modular arithmetic, non-Abelian group algebra, compositional generalization) without the parameter-scaling and grokking instability classical approaches need — a 7th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 6 prior
  - 2026-09-24 (v2 revision; v1 originally posted 2026-02-06, opened this session) — https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06699 — Pecilli, Rosati: "Quantum Attention by Overlap Interference: Predicting Classical and Many-Body Quantum Sequences" — a variational quantum self-attention (QSA) realizing the required nonlinearity through interference of state overlaps and a degree-k polynomial kernel, with a loss based on Rényi-1/2 entropic functionals estimated via two observables' expectation values (avoiding amplitude-to-classical-probability decoding); newly cross-listed to cs.CL/cs.LG in this v2 — a 6th independent group (no author overlap with the trend's prior 5)
  - 2026-09-19 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23016 — Iacopetta, Gasparini: "Watching Quantum Models Think: Hilbert-Space Interpretability in Quantum Transformer Blocks" — tracks quantum mutual information, entanglement entropy and state fidelity through the layers of a fully-coherent Quantum Transformer Block (quantum analogues of both attention and feedforward) to give direct, non-opaque insight into how the model processes information — a 5th independent group (no author overlap with any of the trend's 4 prior), the first interpretability-focused contribution on this trend's own architectures rather than a new application
  - 2026-09-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21115 — Rhrissorrakrai, Bose, Guzman-Saenz, Utro, Pardia (IBM Research): "Hybrid quantum-classical attention for histopathology-based molecular profiling in data-limited cancers" — replaces softmax attention in a transformer for histopathology-based gene-expression prediction with a quantum-derived doubly stochastic matrix (QDSM), evaluated across 29 TCGA cancer cohorts plus an independent CPTAC pancreatic-cancer cohort, with selective gains on the largest cohorts — a 4th independent group (no author overlap with the trend's founding 3), clearing the confidence note's own flagged promotion bar (STAGE PROMOTED seed → emerging this run), and the first entrant from a major industrial research lab (IBM)
  - 2026-10-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34492 — Liu, Jun: "Wave Is All You Need: A Quantum Language Model Based on Fully Connected Quantum Boltzmann Machine" — implements a self-attention mechanism via unitary evolution of a fully-connected quantum Boltzmann machine, implicitly encoding positional information and attention without decomposing semantic streams into discrete matrices — an 11th independent group (CAP-SWAP)

### [id: trend-017] QML/QEC security: adversarial attacks on and defenses for quantum-classical ML components

alias: qml-qec-security
stage: dormant
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-09-24
last_evidence: 2026-10-19
evidence:
  - 2026-10-19 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26240 — El Maouaki, Izen, Innan, Marchisio, Shafique: "Q-MALDetect: Quantum Image-Based Malware Detection and Adversarial Robustness Analysis" — a hybrid convolutional/variational-quantum detector for byte-image representations of x86-64 ELF binaries reaches 97.2% training accuracy, but robustness deteriorates under feature-space and Grad-CAM-guided executable-aware evasion attacks (up to 78.7% attack success rate at the strongest tested feature-space attack) — a 7th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 6 prior, extending the adversarial-robustness strand to a security-critical application (malware detection) rather than a generic classifier benchmark
  - 2026-10-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19077 — Ding, Huang, Xing: "Federated Unlearning of Quantum Data via Classical Shadows" — proposes SHADE, in which each federated-learning node records a classical shadow of every training state at training time (since a consumed/measured quantum state cannot be replayed or cloned to support later unlearning), estimates the QNN gradient/curvature on forget-data from the shadows and removes their effect via a federated Newton correction, with proven shadow-size bounds for a target unlearning error — a 6th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 5 prior, broadening the PRIVACY strand (data erasure/right-to-be-forgotten for quantum-classical ML) alongside 2610.17145's blind-delegation privacy angle
  - 2026-10-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17145 — Fleur, Propp, Feld: "On the Computational Complexity of Blind Quantum Machine Learning" — constructs MasonQ, a universal brickwork transpiler bridging universal blind quantum computation (UBQC) with QML algorithms (HHL, quantum recommendation systems), proving new upper bounds on UBQC brickwork-graph depth and showing quantum computational-efficiency and privacy advantages can coexist for QML workloads delegated to an untrusted server — a 5th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 4 prior, the first evidence item on this trend's QML-classifier-security strand addressing PRIVACY (delegated/blind computation) rather than adversarial robustness or poisoning
  - 2026-09-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38720 — Zhang, Zhao, Cui, Heng, Chu, Blanken: "Does the Readout Bypass Leak the Input? A Feature-Visibility Audit of Hybrid Quantum-Classical Models" — audits readout-side residual hybrids that concatenate raw inputs with measured quantum features, showing that under single-example gradient sharing a biased first linear layer admits standard analytic recovery of its input — the bypass exposes raw coordinates without needing to invert the quantum circuit itself; a visibility-conditioned privacy audit across two tabular datasets and four architectures (median full-record PSNR 73-96 dB for residual/input-only heads; a loss-threshold membership attack stays near chance) — a 4th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 3 prior, clearing the seed→emerging "multi-source, forming" bar
  - 2026-09-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19090 — Özgüler: "Securing quantum error correction against misleading advice from AI agents" — identifies that opposite coherent-rotation errors on an odd-distance square toric code can produce identical passive syndrome-history distributions, an ambiguity a malicious or mistaken AI adviser could exploit to recommend a harmful recovery update, then shows additional calibration measurements support certified, ambiguity-resistant recovery decisions under drift
  - 2026-08-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27805 — Li, Peng, Chen, Wang: "Decoder-Prior Poisoning in Quantum Error Correction: Attacks and PriorGuard Defense" — identifies the prior-update path of calibration-aware surface-code decoders (which set matching-graph edge probabilities from recent device behavior) as an overlooked integrity-critical attack surface: a poisoned prior can change decoding decisions even with an unchanged syndrome stream, logical labels and decoder implementation; proposes a PriorGuard defense (a backlog item, submitted Aug-18, surfaced this session via the day's arXiv sweep)
  - 2026-09-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11637 — Guan, Huang: "Certifying Adversarial Robustness of Quantum Classifiers under Known-Readout Query Access" — a measurement-only certification framework giving provable robustness/attack-detection guarantees for quantum classifiers queried only via finite-shot readout, with no access to internal evolution, parameters or gradients
notes: Created `seed` via the daily convergence check 2026-09-24 — 3 independent groups (Guan/Huang; Özgüler; Li/Peng/Chen/Wang), no author overlap, hold opened primary artifacts on adversarial security for quantum-classical ML components — attacks on and defenses for quantum classifiers AND for the classical ML/statistical components (calibration-aware decoder priors) inside quantum error correction — within Sep-10 → Sep-16 (Li et al.'s own Aug-18 posting predates the window but was only discovered this session, a genuine backlog catch). ### [id: trend-018] Sample-based Quantum Diagonalization (SQD): extending a hybrid sampling-diagonalization technique beyond its chemistry origins

alias: sample-based-quantum-diagonalization
stage: emerging
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-10-30
last_evidence: 2026-11-11
evidence:
  - 2026-11-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16080 — Mishu, Jiang, Heidari, Zhang, Chen: "TGen-SQD: Physics-Informed Transferable Configuration Generator for Sample-Based Quantum Diagonalization" — existing ML configuration selectors for SQD use task-specific cues and retrain per Hamiltonian; TGen-SQD is a physics-informed generator jointly trained ACROSS multiple Hamiltonians from configuration-interaction-weighted data, using orbital- and system-level features — a 6th independent group, no author/org overlap with any of the trend's prior 5, opened this session via today's arXiv quant-ph∩cs.ET sweep
  - 2026-11-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13755 — Kumar, Keithley, Iwamori, Kanno: "A Hardware-Trained Generative Quantum Eigensolver for Sample-Based Quantum Diagonalization of Organotin Hydroxides up to 70 Qubits" — trains generative quantum eigensolver (GQE) policies (transformers conditioned on molecular descriptors) to generate SQD state-preparation circuits, first on a noiseless simulator and then with the real IBM Heron `ibm_kobe` processor inside the training loop at 46 and 70 qubits, admitting candidates only past a classical-CCSD(T)-referenced gate — a 5th independent group, opened this session via today's arXiv sweep, and the trend's first genuinely ML-ASSISTED SQD variant (a trained generative policy rather than a fixed sampling/fragmentation scheme), exactly the "learning/ML-assisted SQD variant" the trend's own creation note flagged as a future watch signal linking it to trend-002/012's hardware/circuit-synthesis axes
  - 2026-11-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04496 — Kumar, Shirakawa, Kim, Wakizaka, Hamamura, Sato: "Broken-Symmetry Reference States for Sample-Based Quantum Diagonalization with Near-DMRG Accuracy" — a broken-symmetry unrestricted-Hartree-Fock starting point for SQD, lowering the mean-field reference energy by ~534 mHa on a 54-electron/88-qubit iron-sulfur cubane benchmark — a 4th independent group, no author overlap with any of the trend's 3 prior
  - 2026-10-31 — https://quantumcomputingreport.com/los-alamos-and-qc-ware-model-correlated-iron-oxides-using-sample-based-quantum-diagonalization-on-ibm-hardware — Los Alamos National Laboratory + QC Ware (technical writeup, opened this session): couples ab initio-informed d-p tight-binding models with SQD on IBM Quantum's `ibm_miami` to reach chemical accuracy on diatomic/dimer iron-oxide clusters (FeO, Fe2O2) where standalone VQE optimization struggled — the trend's first REAL-HARDWARE demonstration, and a 3rd independent group
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41986 — Pradeep, Chaudhary, Patra et al.: "Resource efficient Sample Based Quantum Simulation through Fragmentation and Virtual Partitioning of QPU" — FMO-QVM-HCI-HSQD fragments a large quantum-chemistry problem and runs fragments on virtual QPU regions via a half-qubit ansatz, demonstrated on ibm_marrakesh/ibm_boston up to 52 qubits — a 2nd independent group
  - 2026-10-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41708 — Selim: "Extending Sample-based Quantum Diagonalization to Systems of Linear Equations" — extends IBM's SQD technique from quantum chemistry to solving Ax=b via a sample-based Krylov variant, proving subspace-dimension guarantees while avoiding HHL's phase-estimation/post-selection requirements — the founding item
notes: Created `seed` via the weekly convergence check 2026-11-07 (W45), formalizing the "SQD-cluster-watch" flag the 11-02/11-03/11-04 dailies carried forward rather than resolving mid-daily. 4 independent groups (Selim; Pradeep/Chaudhary/Patra et al.; Los Alamos/QC Ware; Kumar/Shirakawa/Kim/Wakizaka/Hamamura/Sato), no author/org overlap, hold opened primary artifacts extending IBM's Sample-based Quantum Diagonalization (SQD) technique — originally a quantum-chemistry ground-state method using a QPU purely as a Fock-space sampler for a classical diagonalization step — beyond its chemistry origins (linear-algebra solvers, QPU fragmentation/virtual-partitioning for larger problems, real-hardware strongly-correlated-materials modeling, improved reference states), all within Oct-30 → Nov-3, clearing the ≥3-group convergence bar this ledger uses to create a trend. Scope placement: axis-2 (enabling quantum-computing research — quantum algorithms & complexity), NOT axis-1/lead-axis QML — none of the 4 items has a trained ML/learning component; the precedent for a non-ML axis-2/3 trend is already established by trend-006 (dequantization/honest-baselines) and trend-011 (provable learning separations), so this does not require an ML angle to qualify. Created at `seed`/`low` (4 groups just clears the ≥3 bar, no sustained multi-week cadence yet to judge); watch for a 5th independent group and whether a learning/ML-assisted SQD variant appears, which would also make it relevant to trend-002/trend-012's hardware/circuit-synthesis axes. Distinct from trend-005 (Neural Quantum States, classical NN ansätze for wavefunctions) and trend-014 (learning Hamiltonian/dissipative-rate parameters from data) — SQD uses the QPU as a sampler for a classical post-processing diagonalization, not a trained model. Daily (2026-11-11): 5th independent group appended — Kumar/Keithley/Iwamori/Kanno's hardware-trained generative-quantum-eigensolver paper (2611.13755, Nov-10, opened this session via today's arXiv sweep), not cap-blocked (5/10). last_evidence 2026-11-03 → 2026-11-10. This is the trend's first ML-ASSISTED variant (a trained GQE/transformer policy generating SQD circuits, with the real IBM Heron `ibm_kobe` processor inside the training loop) — the exact future-watch signal flagged at creation; also makes this trend newly relevant to trend-002/012's hardware-training/circuit-synthesis axes. Held `seed`/`low` (still short of the 4-5-groups-over-sustained-weeks precedent for a confidence raise; stage/confidence changes are weekly-only). Daily (2026-11-13) — STAGE PROMOTED seed → emerging: appended Mishu/Jiang/Heidari/Zhang/Chen's TGen-SQD paper (2611.16080, Nov-11, opened this session via today's arXiv sweep) as a 6th independent group, no author/org overlap with any of the prior 5 — clearing the same ≥4-independent-groups "multi-source, forming" bar used for trend-016's own seed→emerging promotion, and a 2nd ML-assisted SQD variant (joint cross-Hamiltonian training of a configuration generator, distinct from the 11-10 GQE/transformer-policy item's per-Hamiltonian training). last_evidence 2026-11-10 → 2026-11-11. Confidence held `low` (confidence changes weekly-only; no sustained multi-week corroboration yet on a single concrete claim). Weekly recalibration (2026-11-14 W46): confidence RAISED low → high — 6 independent groups (no author/org overlap) now corroborate the SQD-extensions sub-theme over 2+ weeks (Oct-30 founding batch through the 11-13 TGen-SQD addition), clearing the Hard rules' "≥2 independent authoritative primary sources corroborate on concrete artifacts" bar comfortably.

## study_shelf

Single strong items worth knowing, newest first (format: `date — [name](url) — one line of
why`). The trend bar does NOT apply here; opened primary sources only.

- 2026-11-13 — [Multi-head Quantum Neural Networks: Replacing Transformer Blocks with NISQ Modules inside a 30-Billion-Parameter LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15250) — Amoussou, Aizpurua, Singh, Orús, Jacobsen: the first reported swap of a quantum module for transformer blocks INSIDE an already-pretrained 30B-parameter LLM, not a from-scratch toy model — directly on this radar's quantum-AI lead axis.
- 2026-11-12 — [An unconditional distribution learning advantage with shallow quantum circuits](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01370-4) — Pirnay, Jerbi, Seifert, Eisert (npj Quantum Information, Nov 10): proves a genuine PAC distribution-learning separation for constant-depth quantum circuits over constant-depth classical circuits, by uplifting a known shallow-circuit SAMPLING advantage into a hyperplane-learning problem — worth knowing as a rare UNCONDITIONAL (not just conjectured or asymptotic) advantage result stated directly in a learning-theory framework, from a QML-specialist author team.
- 2026-11-12 — [Simulating Gaussian boson sampling on graphs in polynomial time](https://doi.org/10.22331/q-2026-11-10-2268) — Anand, Chen, Cryan, Freifeld, Goldberg, Guo, Zhang (Quantum, Nov 10): shows that the distribution most graph-theoretic/molecular-similarity GBS applications actually sample from admits a polynomial-time classical sampler — worth knowing as a clean, practically actionable dequantization result for anyone evaluating a GBS-based quantum-advantage claim on a GRAPH application specifically (as opposed to the generic GBS hardness results that don't apply to this common use case).
- 2026-11-11 — [A Hardware-Trained Generative Quantum Eigensolver for Sample-Based Quantum Diagonalization of Organotin Hydroxides up to 70 Qubits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13755) — Kumar, Keithley, Iwamori, Kanno: trains a transformer-based circuit-generating policy with a REAL IBM Heron processor (`ibm_kobe`) inside the training loop at 46/70 qubits, admitting only candidates that clear a classical-CCSD(T)-referenced gate — worth knowing as the first genuinely ML-assisted SQD variant, bridging this radar's SQD and hardware-in-the-loop-training watch areas.
- 2026-11-11 — [Machine-learning decoders for fusion-based quantum computing in real time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13630) — Barter, Bridgeman, Dawson, Farrelly: PRISM, a transformer-based decoder built to exploit known erasure locations in photonic fusion-based fault-tolerant protocols — worth knowing as the first ML-decoder result on a non-superconducting, non-qLDPC platform, extending the QEC-tooling frontier to photonics.

- 2026-11-10 — [Pasqal Demonstrates Logical Qubits Outperform Physical Qubits Solving Differential Equations](https://www.pasqal.com/newsroom/pasqal-demonstrates-logical-qubits-outperform-physical-qubits-solving-differential-equations-an-industry-first-for-neutral-atom-quantum-computing) — Pasqal (Mathiot, Garnaoui, Leriche, Philip, Henriet et al.; arXiv:2605.21276, orig. posted May 20): a quantum-kernel-method differential-equation solver run at both the physical and logical level on a real neutral-atom processor, with the logical implementation outperforming its physical counterpart end-to-end — not on an isolated sub-routine — and specific noise sources traced via the encoding itself. Worth knowing as a concrete, application-level answer to "does error correction actually pay off for a real ML task yet," a ~6-month-old backlog item surfaced this run by chasing an unverified investor-relations PR claim back to its technical primary; also trend-015 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 20th independent group).
- 2026-11-06 — [A proof of the Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture](https://psi.inc/research/aaronson-ambainis/) — Physical Superintelligence (Turturean, Halverson et al.; arXiv:2611.09773): a PSI research system running a non-public Anthropic model found and Lean-verified a full proof of the 17-year-old Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture, independently confirmed by outside experts (Guy Blanc, MIT; Jordan Docter/Carmen Strassle/Li-Yang Tan, Stanford) and publicly endorsed by Scott Aaronson himself — settles that superpolynomial quantum query advantage on random/unstructured inputs requires a promise/structure, exactly as conjectured; worth knowing both for the result itself and as a landmark case study in AI-driven resolution of a named, long-standing open problem in quantum complexity theory. HYPE-SKEPTIC NOTE: does not affect Shor's algorithm or chemistry/physics-simulation advantage claims — only random-oracle decision-problem query advantage, a case already widely believed false; also trend-009 evidence (CAP-SWAP).
- 2026-11-06 — [The Compendium of Super-Quadratic Computational Quantum Advantages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32595) — Kammerlander, O'Brien, King, Somma, Babbush et al. (Open Quantum Institute/CERN + Google Quantum AI + Microsoft + JPMorganChase + MIT + Harvard, Oct 23, a capture-leak catch found via today's digest sweep): a consensus, structured enumeration of 24 computational problems with credible evidence for more-than-quadratic quantum speedup, characterizing the real-world structural constraints (data loading, error correction) that must hold to realize each one — a disciplined, multi-lab reference map for exactly the "is this advantage real and under what conditions" question this radar tracks; also trend-011 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 19th independent group).
- 2026-11-06 — [Quantum Knowledge Distillation: State-of-the-art, Applications and Open Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08461) — Al-Sulaiti, Chen, Dastagir, Farouk, Al-Kuwari (Nov 5): a comprehensive survey of knowledge distillation applied to quantum machine learning, organized along two axes (teacher-student configuration and the role of quantum resources) spanning compression, cross-architecture transfer, circuit approximation, distributed learning and classical deployment of quantum-derived models — a useful map of a fast-fragmenting sub-literature.
- 2026-11-06 — [Quantum-Classical Auxiliary-Field Quantum Monte Carlo at the Edge of Practicability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19239) — IQM + Volkswagen Group Innovation (hardware-validated Nov-5 via a QCR technical writeup, orig. posted Jun-2026): an order-of-magnitude classical-runtime reduction (up to 124x at 50 orbitals) for QC-AFQMC via Aitken's block transformation on singular Pfaffians, validated on a 54-qubit superconducting processor — a concrete, non-ML algorithmic answer to the same measurement/post-processing bottleneck two independent ML-based papers tackled the same week (see trend-005).
- 2026-11-05 — [Reimagining computational quantum research with AI scientists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02833) — Du, Zhao, Wang, Lu, Zhang, Tao (Nov 2): introduces OmniQ, benchmarking AI scientists on reproducing 59 published computational-quantum-science papers — a sobering, directly actionable read for any researcher weighing how far to trust an AI-generated reproduction of their own or others' quantum results.
- 2026-11-05 — [GFlowQEC: Generative Flow Networks that Learn to Sample Quantum LDPC Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06819) — Batista, Falcao, Machado (Nov 4): the first generative flow network for quantum code design, learning to propose good qLDPC codes in proportion to a reward under a tight per-evaluation search budget — a clean new tool-design pattern for anyone searching a huge discrete code space with expensive evaluations.
- 2026-11-04 — [FirstQFM Demonstrates Quantum Reservoir Computing on Rigetti's 108-Qubit Processor to Outperform Classical Financial Benchmarks](https://quantumcomputingreport.com/firstqfm-demonstrates-quantum-reservoir-computing-on-rigettis-108-qubit-processor-to-outperform-classical-financial-benchmarks/) — FirstQFM + SW7 Group + Rigetti (Nov 3): a blinded out-of-sample financial-forecasting pilot on a real 108-qubit Cepheus-1-108Q beats a pooled MLP, PCR/PLS regression, Random Forest and Amazon's Chronos-2 zero-shot foundation model on directional accuracy, P&L and Sharpe ratio across 43 assets and 600 trading dates, while the authors explicitly disclaim general quantum advantage. Worth knowing as a rare honest, real-hardware QRC-vs-classical head-to-head that names its own limits rather than overselling them; also trend-008 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 24th independent group).
- 2026-11-04 — [Comparing classical and quantum QUBO solvers: optimality, trajectory diagnostics, and limits of solver selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35126) — Hellstern, Schacht, Zaefferer (Oct 26): benchmarks classical, tensor-network and fixed-schedule quantum-annealing QUBO solvers across 180+ instances, finding a general-purpose classical branch-and-cut solver matches or beats the quantum approach on the large majority of them. Worth knowing as a disciplined, broad-coverage honest baseline for anyone evaluating a quantum-annealing optimization claim; also trend-006 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 24th independent group).
- 2026-11-03 — [Demonstration of meter-scale L-couplers between two IBM Quantum Nighthawk processors](https://www.ibm.com/quantum/blog/connecting-quantum-processors-with-l-couplers) — Sundaresan et al., IBM (paper Oct 21 / blog Oct 27): four L-couplers connect two 120-qubit Nighthawk processors with 99% average quantum-state-transfer fidelity, the first multi-coupler modular-interconnect design compatible with scaling toward IBM's fault-tolerant Starling architecture. Worth knowing as the concrete engineering step that turns "modular quantum computing" from a roadmap slide into a measured fidelity number.
- 2026-11-03 — [Electronic-Structure-Informed Many-Body Encoding for Quantum Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40840) — Zhang, Culpitt, Yin, Zhu, Li (Oct 29): a physics-informed quantum encoding that maps n-body reduced-density-matrix descriptors onto n-qubit gates, showing prediction accuracy for electron-correlation energies improves systematically with encoding body-order toward the full-CI limit. Worth knowing as a rare quantum-feature-map design principle grounded in the physics of the data rather than a generic/ansatz-driven encoding choice; also trend-015 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 18th independent group).
- 2026-10-30 — [The Quartic Gap Between Quantum and Dequantized Algorithms for Linear Algebra Is Tight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42069) — Chakrabarti, Herman, Kim, Ozgul (Oct 30): proves the known $\widetilde O(ρ)$-vs-$\widetilde O(ρ^4)$ quantum-vs-dequantized query-complexity gap for QRAM-accessed linear systems is TIGHT up to a log factor, by constructing, for every fixed condition number, dense linear systems where the dequantized algorithm genuinely needs the full $ρ^4$ queries. Worth knowing as a rare case where dequantization research produces a matching LOWER bound rather than just a faster classical algorithm — it tells a QML researcher exactly how far "just dequantize it" can ever go for this problem class, not merely that it currently falls short; also trend-006 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 23rd independent group).
- 2026-10-30 — [Distilling Knowledge from Search: Decoder Model Factory for Quantum LDPC Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41398) — Bilgin, Hou, Srinivas, You (Oct 30): GraphMP, a message-passing qLDPC decoder that TRANSFERS without fine-tuning across code families, sizes, error rates and noise models, trained via a "decoder model factory" — successive generations of the decoder are trained on experience (knowledge distilled from model-guided beam search) generated by the PRIOR generation's own decoding runs, folding compute spent decoding into reusable training data. Worth knowing as a reusable training PARADIGM (self-improving decoder generations), not just another single decoder result; also trend-001 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 12th independent group).
- 2026-10-29 — [Collaborative AI agents for autonomous experimental research with solid-state quantum spins](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40589) — Mansouri, Kimura, Koga, Arai (Oct 29): multiple LLM-based agents assess one another's work and can stop or redirect investigations — generating and rejecting their own research questions, requiring separate theoretical checks, and designing experiments specifically to challenge their own claims — demonstrated end-to-end on real ensembles of NV-diamond quantum spins, deriving and then computationally verifying the minimum calibration settings needed to predict microwave-pulse-sequence responses. Worth knowing as a concrete example of adversarial self-review built INTO an autonomous-lab-automation pipeline, rather than a single agent executing a fixed plan; also trend-013 evidence (10th independent group, cap reached).
- 2026-10-29 — [Co-Innovating with IonQ: How One Utility Is Solving Power Grid Complexity](https://ionq.com/blog/co-innovating-with-ionq-how-one-utility-is-solving-power-grid-complexity) — IonQ + EPB (Oct 28): three companion papers tackle the same real utility's distribution-network-reconfiguration problem with three distinct quantum methods — hybrid quantum-classical deep RL with decision-head masking ([2609.40327](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40327)), LR-QAOA-guided MISOCP ([2609.40264](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40264)), and warm-start iterative QITE with radiality-preserving encoding ([2609.39602](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39602)) — all validated on real grid topologies on IonQ Forte Enterprise under a "Utility-in-the-Loop" protocol. Worth knowing as a rare coordinated three-method head-to-head on one real industrial optimization problem with real-hardware validation, rather than a single isolated demo; the RL paper is also this run's trend-002-adjacent re-surfacing (previously dropped as a narrow single-application result, now stronger in context).
- 2026-10-28 — [Five results on Open Quantum Problems](https://psi.inc/research/oqp-batch-2026/) — PSI (Physical Superintelligence, Oct 23): PSI's own AI research system produces new results on five open problems in quantum information theory — an entanglement-distillability bound for Werner states, a locality bound on CHSH violation by vacuum correlations, optimal CGLMP-violating measurement strategies, a tight bound on how many PPT maps suffice to break entanglement, and a counterexample to a causal-process-extension conjecture — each released with a paper, a Lean formalization and verification code. Worth knowing as a vivid instance of an AI system producing new, machine-verified mathematics in quantum information theory itself, not just reasoning about circuits or code; also trend-009 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 23rd independent group).
