- 2026-10-27 — [Emergent Recursive Kernels in Quantum Reservoir Computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34430) — Ce Wang (Oct 25): proves that a quantum reservoir with a fixed input-coupling-plus-Haar-unitary evolution admits an exact recursive kernel in the large-dimension limit, reducing each kernel evaluation to a scalar recurrence linear in history length and independent of reservoir size — the field's most rigorous mechanistic account yet of why fixed-dynamics QRC kernels behave as they do; also trend-008 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 22nd independent group).

Weekly recalibration (2026-10-10 W41): pruned 8 picks older than 30 days (dated 2026-09-03
through 2026-09-09), each already preserved in its own day's report; the picks below (newest
from 2026-09-10) remain.

- 2026-10-26 — [QuantumMatterExplorer: An Autonomous Agent for the Discovery of Quantum Many-Body Phases of Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32001) — Nys, Jreissaty, Carrasquilla (Oct 22): an LLM-based autonomous agent that takes only a Hamiltonian and its parameter ranges and returns a full phase diagram from its own numerical simulations, reasoning over a long horizon with a persistent written plan and driving density-matrix-renormalization-group/variational tools itself. Worth knowing as a vivid, reusable instance of agentic AI directly doing computational quantum-many-body research (choosing where to compute, not just analyzing results already computed) from a leading NQS/ML-for-quantum-matter researcher (Carrasquilla); also trend-009 evidence (CAP-SWAP, 22nd independent group).

- 2026-10-23 — [Lean-QuantumAlg: A Framework for AI Agents to Produce Quantum Algorithms that Humans Can Trust](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30216) — Zhang, Jing, Zhao, Liu, Xin Wang (Oct 21): a Lean 4 contract binds correctness, success probability, borrowed-state restoration and resource bounds to a single machine-checked execution, demonstrated on nine AI-formalized algorithms from Grover search to quantum attacks on RSA-2048/P-256 with under 1% statement-level human review once the shared framework is built. Worth knowing as a concrete, reusable template for trusting AI-generated quantum algorithms rather than hoping they are correct; trend-009-adjacent (same group already in evidence, queued rather than appended).
- 2026-10-23 — [Announcing the verifiable quantum circuit compilation competition on Kaggle](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/10/announcing-verifiable-quantum-circuit-compilation-competition-on-kaggle.html) — Khattar, Babbush (Google Quantum AI, Oct 21): a public Kaggle competition built on the same verifiable-structure agentic-compilation approach as the pair's own paper (already trend-009 evidence) — a practical on-ramp for researchers to try agentic quantum-circuit compilation themselves rather than only read about it.
- 2026-10-22 — [Decoding quantum errors with NVIDIA Ising Decoding on AWS](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/quantum-computing/decoding-quantum-errors-with-nvidia-ising-decoding-on-aws/) — Huang, Reshef, Rodenbach, Pakrasi, Takeshita (AWS + NVIDIA, Oct 21): trains and benchmarks NVIDIA's open-source Ising Decoding AI pre-decoder across 5 GPU generations (A100 through B200), then shows it cuts per-round QEC decoding latency 2.4x-2.9x over MWPM alone at distance 31 while improving the logical error rate. Worth knowing as a concrete, reproducible playbook (with cost-per-GPU-generation figures) for anyone standing up their own AI-decoder training pipeline; also trend-002 evidence (CAP-SWAP).
- 2026-10-21 — [Mamba Quantum Eigensolver (MQE): A Selective State Space Model for Scalable Ground State Simulations in Quantum Chemistry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29779) — Petronilo, Araújo, Teixeira, Cruz: swaps the Generative Quantum Eigensolver's Transformer backbone for a Mamba selective-state-space model, trading quadratic attention cost for linear-time sequence generation of ground-state-search ansätze. Worth knowing as a sign that the generative-circuit-synthesis literature is already absorbing the classical-ML field's post-Transformer architecture shift; also trend-012 evidence (CAP-SWAP).
- 2026-10-21 — [Does Data Complexity Predict Quantum Advantage? A framework, a pre-specified test, and an attribution of the measured edge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16410) — Pere (v2, Oct 7; orig. Sep 2025): a pre-specified, budget-matched test across 27 datasets finds data complexity alone does not predict the quantum edge, and that the apparent signal tracks classical-baseline weakness rather than a robust quantum contribution. Worth knowing as a disciplined template for how to pre-commit a falsifiable quantum-advantage test before running it, rather than rationalizing results after the fact; also trend-006 evidence (20th independent group, a dormancy-probe capture-leak catch).
- 2026-10-20 — [Advancing Fault-Tolerant Quantum Computing with GPU-Accelerated Simulation](https://nvidia.github.io/cuda-quantum/blogs/blog/2026/10/19/jupiter-magic-state-simulation/) — Holmes, Kloss, Pancotti, Svore (NVIDIA + Jülich Supercomputing Centre, Oct 19): simulates magic-state preparation in a 104-qubit protocol using 8,192 GPUs on the JUPITER supercomputer, developed/validated with the NVIDIA Accelerated Quantum Research Center. Worth knowing as a concrete scale yardstick for how far classical GPU simulation can validate fault-tolerance protocols before hardware catches up, from a team including Krysta Svore; also flagged as trend-001's next cap-swap candidate.
- 2026-10-20 — [Large Language Model Steering of Acousto-Optic Tweezer Arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25014) — Sheng, Xiao, Zhang, Wang, Zhang, Geng, Huang, Li (Oct 18): a general-purpose LLM acts as a human-in-the-loop strategy layer guiding a real 10x10 neutral-atom tweezer array through a calibration-strategy transition when standard feedback stalls, with deterministic software doing the analysis/updates and an experimentalist authorizing each step. Worth knowing as a vivid, concrete instance of LLM-as-strategy-layer (not autopilot) for real hardware calibration; also trend-013 evidence (9th independent group).
- 2026-10-19 — [Leveraging verifiable structure in quantum circuits to find more efficient compilations with agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23832) — Khattar, Rubin, Gidney, Broughton, Low, Babbush (Google Quantum AI, Oct 16): AI agents compile quantum circuits more efficiently by restricting to bottleneck subroutines with classically-verifiable structure, enabling efficient certification of agent-proposed designs. Worth knowing alongside the Gu/Kemeny/Yelin/Lukin circuit-discovery paper (already trend-012 evidence, also publicized the same week on Anthropic's own research blog) as a 2nd major-lab demonstration that agentic AI can find genuinely better quantum circuits, not just automate existing search; also trend-009 evidence (CAP-SWAP).
- 2026-10-19 — [Review Evaluates AI and Quantum Computing Paradigms for Adaptive Combinatorial Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11759) — Hoong Chuin Lau (Oct 13): a 119-paper survey organizing the AI+quantum-optimization literature into AI-for-Quantum-Optimization, Quantum-for-AI, and AI-Quantum Co-Optimization, concluding the first paradigm currently shows the most promise. Worth knowing as a field-level map for a researcher trying to place a given AI/QC-optimization paper within the broader landscape.
- 2026-10-16 — [Magic state cultivation on a superconducting quantum processor](https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/models-and-research/quantum-computing/magic-state-cultivation-quantum-computing) — Google Quantum AI (Rosenfeld, Gidney et al.; published in Science, Oct 15): the first physical hardware demonstration that magic-state cultivation works in practice, cutting T-state error 40x and reaching 99.99% logical fidelity on the Willow processor. Worth knowing as a flagship resolution of a long-standing FTQC resource bottleneck (cheap, high-fidelity non-Clifford T-gates), not a simulation or proposal; also trend-001 evidence (CAP-SWAP).
- 2026-10-16 — [Bubble-Sort Exchange Networks: Symmetry Selection Rules and Language-Model Distillation on Exchange-Only Spin Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22093) — Minato (Oct 15): distils GPT-2's own next-token output distributions into a small variational quantum Born machine built from exchange-only spin-qubit SWAP networks, beating a bond-dimension-4 classical MPS baseline on KL divergence, and shows the scheme's "leakage" out of the logical code space is a resource rather than an error. Worth knowing as a concrete, quantitative example of distilling a classical LLM's own distribution into a trainable quantum generative circuit — a different direction from the usual "quantum circuit generates classical samples" framing.
- 2026-10-15 — [From Minutes to Seconds: Faster Decoder Recalibration for Quantum Error Correction](https://www.riverlane.com/news/faster-decoder-recalibration-for-quantum-error-correction) — Riverlane (Gransden, Varbanov, Hübotter, Kashyap; Oct 14, opened this session): moves QEC decoder-weight recalibration's dominant cost (decoding thousands-to-millions of syndromes per training epoch) onto a single NVIDIA H100, cutting one epoch from ~3 minutes on a CPU core to 2.8 seconds — a genuinely useful, concretely-benchmarked practical speedup for anyone running real-time decoder calibration loops, and this radar's first evidence item from Riverlane, a dedicated QEC-decoder company.
